## Implementation plan

# Issue #213: remove Maintenance Mode

Source: https://github.com/laststance/gitbox/issues/213

Owner's direction (2026-10-12): finish in one PR, nothing parked in TODOS.md when it can be done now, scope is not a constraint, data loss is acceptable (hobby product, 2 users), aggressive changes without asking, full code review and design review, then deploy and verify on production as `ryota-murakami`.

## Problem

Maintenance Mode is a second, parallel place to keep a repository: its own table, page, dialogs, server actions, RPCs, RLS path and tests. A board named "Maintenance" does the same job with the features boards already have (columns, drag, notes, links, comments, Move to Another Board). The parallel path costs code and rules: `projectinfo` has two owners, the picker checks two places, and "board vs Maintenance exclusivity" is enforced in app code only.

## Goal

GitBox has one place for a repository: a card on a board. The Maintenance page, table, RPCs, actions, UI entries, tests and docs are gone. The owner's archive is kept: every Maintenance entry becomes a card on a new board named "Maintenance". The migration refuses to run while an entry cannot become a card (its repository is already on a board, or two entries differ only in letter case); production has none (audit below).

What the Maintenance page had and a board does not: search by name, sorting and a list view. The issue accepts that trade. On a desktop browser, find-in-page reaches every card on a board (a column renders all of its cards). In the installed PWA there is no find-in-page; the user scrolls the column. Repository search across boards is already in TODOS.md and is the real fix.

Success:

- `git grep -i maintenance -- src e2e mocks supabase/seed.sql eslint.config.mjs knip.config.ts README.md CLAUDE.md SPEC.md TODOS.md` returns no lines.
- `public/screenshots/app-screenshot.png` and `social-preview.png` no longer show a "Maintenance Mode" sidebar entry.
- On ordinary boards a repository can be parked, found again and brought back with its note: on the local copy of production, find a card on the "Maintenance" board, open its note, move it to a working board and back. A user with one board creates the destination board inside the move dialog and finishes the move there.
- Production after rollout: table `maintenance` and column `projectinfo.maintenance_id` do not exist; the owner has a board "Maintenance" whose card count equals the `maintenance` row count taken right before the merge (23 on 2026-10-12); the `projectinfo` rows that lived on Maintenance entries (2 on 2026-10-12) open from their cards; the migrated cards whose repository GitHub still returns show a description.

## Current state

Audit on production, counts only (2026-10-12): 23 `maintenance` rows, all of 1 user; 2 `projectinfo` rows linked through `maintenance_id`; 0 repositories both on a board and in Maintenance; 0 case-variant duplicates; 0 entries whose id is also the id of a card; 2 policies on `projectinfo`; 0 boards whose name contains "maintenance"; 5 boards, 27 cards, 2 users.

The audit query (the same comparison the migration uses; run again in section 6, step 5):

```sql
SELECT
  (SELECT count(*) FROM maintenance) AS entries,
  (SELECT count(DISTINCT user_id) FROM maintenance) AS users_with_entries,
  (SELECT count(*) FROM projectinfo WHERE maintenance_id IS NOT NULL) AS projectinfo_on_entries,
  (SELECT count(*) FROM maintenance m JOIN repocard r ON r.user_id = m.user_id
     AND lower(r.repo_owner) = lower(m.repo_owner)
     AND lower(r.repo_name) = lower(m.repo_name)) AS entries_also_on_a_board,
  (SELECT count(*) FROM (SELECT 1 FROM maintenance
     GROUP BY user_id, lower(repo_owner), lower(repo_name)
     HAVING count(*) > 1) d) AS case_variant_groups,
  (SELECT count(*) FROM maintenance m JOIN repocard r ON r.id = m.id)
     AS entries_sharing_an_id_with_a_card,
  (SELECT count(*) FROM pg_policies
     WHERE schemaname = 'public' AND tablename = 'projectinfo') AS projectinfo_policies;
```

Where Maintenance lives today:

- DB: table `maintenance` (`unique_repo_per_user`, case-sensitive), `projectinfo.maintenance_id` (FK, unique, exclusive CHECK with `repo_card_id`, which is nullable because of it), two RLS policies on `projectinfo` with a maintenance path, functions `move_to_maintenance` and `restore_to_board`.
- Server: `moveToMaintenance`, `restoreToBoard` (`src/lib/actions/repo-cards.ts`), `src/lib/actions/maintenance-project-info.ts`, `getUserMaintenanceRepoIdentifiers` and `BoardInitialData.maintenanceRepoIdentifiers` (`board-data.ts`), maintenance placement lookup and the `'maintenance'` skip reason (`repo-card-duplicates.ts`), `FkConfig` in `shared-project-info.ts` (exists only to serve two owners), maintenance count on `/account`.
- Client: route `src/app/maintenance/**` (page, layout, loading, error, client, 4 hooks, stories), `RestoreToBoardDialog`, `DeleteMaintenanceDialog`, "Move to Maintenance" in `OverflowMenu` plus its `context`/`onRestoreToBoard`/`onDelete` props and generic `TId`, `onMaintenance` prop chain (`BoardPageClient` → `KanbanBoard` → `SortableColumn`/`StatusColumn` → `RepoCard`), "In Maintenance" rows in `HeldRepositoryList`, maintenance fallback in `AddRepositoryCombobox`, Sidebar entry, Command Palette entry and its `G M` hint, `ROUTES.MAINTENANCE`, `MaintenanceId`/`toMaintenanceId`/`CardIdentifier` brands and their users (`NoteModal`, `useNoteModalDraft`, `draftSlice`), landing "Maintenance Mode" feature card, "Maintenance Items" tile on `/account`.
- Tests and fixtures: 4 `e2e/logged-in/maintenance-*.spec.ts`, maintenance cases in 9 other specs, `MaintenanceClient.test.tsx`, maintenance cases in 16 other unit test files, 11 story files, `mocks/handlers/{data,supabase}.ts`, `supabase/seed.sql`, `e2e/helpers/db-query.ts`, `eslint.config.mjs` and `knip.config.ts` entries.
- Docs: `SPEC.md`, `README.md`, `CLAUDE.md`, `TODOS.md`.

The lists above name the known places. The definition of done is the `git grep` in Success: comments, docstrings, test names and fixture headers that mention Maintenance are changed too.

## Plan

### 1. Migration `20261012090000_remove_maintenance_mode.sql`

The timestamp sorts after `20261011030000`, the newest migration. One `DO` block (atomic however the CLI wraps the file), `lock_timeout` 5s. Messages carry counts only.

A second run after success does nothing: the block starts with `IF to_regclass('public.maintenance') IS NULL THEN RETURN`. A failed run changes nothing (one transaction), so there is no partial state to resume from.

Steps, in this order:

1. Lock `maintenance`, `projectinfo` and `repocard` in `ACCESS EXCLUSIVE` mode.
2. Guard: count the entries whose repository is already on one of the user's boards (compared with `lower()`, the key of `repocard_unique_repo_per_user`), the groups of entries of one user that differ only in letter case, and the entries whose `id` is already the `id` of a card (step 3 gives each card its entry's id; without this count such a database would pass the guard and fail on the primary key with an unlabeled error). When any count is above 0, raise an exception and change nothing. No entry and no note is ever dropped by this migration. The same guard reads the policies of `projectinfo` (step 6) and refuses when they are not the two the repository defines. A refusal says what a reader needs without a repository name (the job log is public): the message starts with the label `remove_maintenance_mode:` and carries the counts, `DETAIL` reads "Nothing was changed.", and `HINT` says what to do: delete the board card or the entry of each conflict, then run the migration again (production: `gh workflow run supabase-production.yml`; a local database: `pnpm db:reset`). The runbook (section 6) holds the query that lists the conflicting rows; it is run by hand, never in CI. The rehearsal keeps each refusal exactly as the CLI prints it; when the CLI shows the message without `DETAIL` or `HINT`, their text is moved into the message.
3. For each user with entries, in this order (the card triggers `set_repocard_user_id` and `check_repocard_status_board` read the board and the column):
   - Board: name `Maintenance`, `position = COALESCE(MAX(position), -1) + 1` over the user's boards. A user who already has a board with that name still gets a new one; board names are not unique.
   - Column: name `Repositories`, default color, `order` 0, grid 0/0. The name is neutral on purpose: "Archived" inside a board named "Maintenance" would suggest a second special state.
   - Cards: one per entry. The card reuses the entry's `id`, keeps `created_at`/`updated_at`, gets `meta = '{}'` (what `restore_to_board` wrote) and `order` 0..n by `updated_at DESC NULLS LAST, id` (the order the Maintenance page showed).
4. Re-point `projectinfo`: `SET repo_card_id = maintenance_id, maintenance_id = NULL` for every row with a `maintenance_id`, in one statement (the exclusive CHECK holds throughout). The trigger `update_projectinfo_updated_at` is disabled immediately before this statement and enabled again immediately after it, so `updated_at` is not rewritten.
5. Drop `move_to_maintenance(uuid, uuid, text, text)` and `restore_to_board(uuid, uuid, uuid, text, text, integer)` with `IF EXISTS`.
6. `projectinfo`: the migrations define two policies, "Users can view their project info" and "Users can manage their project info". The guard of step 2 has already refused when `pg_policies` shows any other set for the table (another count or another name), with the count found and a hint to compare the schema with the repository; a policy the repository does not know is never dropped. Drop these two by name, drop `projectinfo_target_exclusive_check`, drop column `maintenance_id` (takes `projectinfo_maintenance_id_key`, the FK and `idx_projectinfo_maintenance_id` with it), set `repo_card_id NOT NULL`, recreate the two policies with the repocard → board path only (same names, same `(select auth.uid())` form as `20260208_reenable_rls_optimized_policies.sql`; the `FOR ALL` policy keeps a `WITH CHECK` equal to its `USING`).
7. `DROP TABLE maintenance` (its policies, trigger and index go with it). The table is not kept under another name: the dump of section 6, step 5 is the copy.
8. `RAISE NOTICE` with three counts: entries moved, boards created, `projectinfo` rows re-pointed. The notice is a convenience for whoever reads the job log; whether the CLI prints it is seen in the rehearsal, and no step depends on it. The record of what was moved is the manifest (rehearsal step 8 and section 6, steps 5 and 7).

Because the card reuses the entry's `id`, a note draft the browser saved under that id (the persisted `draft` slice) continues on the card.

A second migration, `20261012085900_create_board_with_status_lists.sql`, adds the function of section 2. It sorts before the removal on purpose: when the removal refuses to run, the function is already applied and the new build can create boards.

Type files: edit `src/lib/supabase/types.ts` by hand (it carries hand-written aliases; remove the `maintenance` table, `projectinfo.maintenance_id`, the two functions and the `Maintenance` alias; `repo_card_id` becomes `string`; add the signature of `create_board_with_status_lists`, so its `.rpc()` call is typed). Delete `src/lib/supabase/database.types.ts`: `src/app/maintenance/page.tsx` is its only importer. Remove its entries from `knip.config.ts` and `eslint.config.mjs`.

A user whose only data was Maintenance entries ends up with "Maintenance" as their only board, so the first-board creation on sign-in does not run for them. Accepted.

Rehearsal before the merge, on local Supabase. Every `--linked` command is run only after `supabase/.temp/project-ref` is confirmed to read `zfcwvzazzzsefbkztsyp` (production). The host has no `psql`; SQL goes through `docker exec -i supabase_db_gitbox psql -U postgres`. Local `supabase` commands that reset the stack are run the way `pnpm db:reset` runs them (`cd supabase && . .env && supabase …`), so the local GitHub sign-in keeps its credentials.

The rehearsal and the production run use the same Supabase CLI version. The workflow asks for `latest` today, and that is resolved when the job starts: a release between the rehearsal and the merge, or before a retry, would run a CLI nobody rehearsed. So this PR pins `version:` in `.github/workflows/supabase-production.yml` to the version the rehearsal ran on (the newest release at that time; the local CLI is updated to it first), and the pin stays after the rollout. `CLAUDE.md` says that the pin and the local CLI are raised together. The version goes into the PR body.

Schema comparison, before scenario A. The copy of production in scenario B is data only, so its policies, triggers, constraints and indexes come from the repository's migrations. To know that production has the same ones, `supabase db dump --linked --schema public` and `supabase db dump --local --schema public` (local database at `20261011030000`) are compared with `diff`. For `maintenance`, `projectinfo`, `repocard`, `board`, `statuslist`, their policies, triggers, constraints and indexes, and the two functions, there is no difference. A difference is not worked around: the migration is changed to fit what production has, or the difference is removed first; either is recorded in the PR body.

Scenario A, rows built by hand:

1. `supabase db reset --local --version 20261011030000 --no-seed` (the schema production has today).
2. Insert: a user with two entries and no board; a user with one board, one card and one entry that has `projectinfo`.
3. Guard, overlap: add an entry for the repository of the second user's card. `supabase migration up --local` fails with counts 1, 0 and 0; no board was created, the table is still there. Delete that entry. (The function migration sorts first, so this run applies it and it stays applied.)
4. Guard, the other refusals, one at a time, each undone before the next: an entry that differs from an existing one only in letter case (counts 0, 1 and 0); an entry for another repository that carries the `id` of the second user's card (counts 0, 0 and 1); a third policy on `projectinfo` (the message names 3 policies). Each `supabase migration up --local` fails and changes nothing. The output of every refusal is kept: it shows whether the CLI prints `DETAIL` and `HINT`, and step 5 shows whether it prints the `NOTICE`.
5. `supabase migration up --local` succeeds. Expect: the first user gets `Maintenance` at position 0 with 2 cards; the second user gets `Maintenance` after their board with 1 card that carries the entry's `projectinfo` with an unchanged `updated_at`; the `NOTICE` reads 3 entries, 2 boards, 1 `projectinfo` row.

Scenario B, a copy of production:

6. `supabase db dump --linked --data-only --schema public --file <path>` with the path outside the repository and outside any synced directory. No `auth` data is dumped.
7. Reset as in step 1, insert one stub `auth.users` row per user id the dump references, load the dump.
8. `supabase migration up --local`. Check with count queries: 1 new board `Maintenance`, its card count equals the dumped `maintenance` row count, every `projectinfo` row that was on an entry is on the card with the same id and keeps its `updated_at`, `repocard` lost no row, table `maintenance` and column `maintenance_id` are gone, the two policies exist, `update_projectinfo_updated_at` is enabled (`tgenabled = 'O'`). The `NOTICE` reports the same numbers. Counts are the summary; that nothing was lost is shown row by row. Before the migration a manifest is written from the loaded copy: for every entry its id, owner, repository and timestamps; for every `projectinfo` row on an entry its id, the entry's id, `updated_at` and an `md5` over its content columns; for every card its id, board, column, `order` and `updated_at`. After the migration the same columns are read back by id and the two files are compared with `diff`: every entry is a card with the same id, owner, repository and timestamps; every `projectinfo` row is on the card with the entry's id, with the same hash and `updated_at`; every card that existed before is unchanged; the boards that hold the migrated ids are as many as the users who had entries. The destination boards are found through the card ids, not through the board name.
9. Early return: `supabase migration repair --status reverted 20261012090000 --local`, then `supabase migration up --local` again. The block returns at its first statement and the counts of step 8 are unchanged. (Without the repair the CLI skips the file and the early return is never executed.)

The verification query (rehearsal step 8 and section 6, step 7):

```sql
SELECT
  to_regclass('public.maintenance') IS NULL AS table_gone,
  NOT EXISTS (SELECT 1 FROM information_schema.columns
    WHERE table_schema = 'public' AND table_name = 'projectinfo'
      AND column_name = 'maintenance_id') AS column_gone,
  (SELECT count(*) FROM board WHERE name = 'Maintenance') AS boards_named_maintenance,
  (SELECT count(*) FROM repocard r JOIN board b ON b.id = r.board_id
    WHERE b.name = 'Maintenance') AS cards_on_them,
  (SELECT count(*) FROM projectinfo p JOIN repocard r ON r.id = p.repo_card_id
    JOIN board b ON b.id = r.board_id WHERE b.name = 'Maintenance') AS projectinfo_on_them,
  (SELECT count(*) FROM pg_policies
    WHERE schemaname = 'public' AND tablename = 'projectinfo') AS projectinfo_policies,
  (SELECT tgenabled FROM pg_trigger WHERE tgname = 'update_projectinfo_updated_at'
    AND tgrelid = 'public.projectinfo'::regclass) AS projectinfo_trigger;
```

The query above is the summary an operator reads in a few seconds. The manifest comparison of step 8 is the proof, and section 6 repeats it on production (steps 5 and 7).

The database of scenario B stays in place for the local check and the screenshots (section 6, step 3). `pnpm db:reset` puts the local database back to the seed afterwards; the copy also holds the second user's rows, so it does not stay longer than the check needs.

### 2. Server

- `repo-cards.ts`: delete `moveToMaintenance` and `restoreToBoard`. `getUserBoardsWithStatusLists` stays: Move to Another Board uses it.
- Delete `maintenance-project-info.ts`.
- `shared-project-info.ts` → `project-info-core.ts`: remove `FkConfig`; core functions take the card id and use `repo_card_id` directly. `project-info.ts` stays the `'use server'` boundary. Update every import.
- `board-data.ts`: delete `getUserMaintenanceRepoIdentifiers` and `maintenanceRepoIdentifiers`; `board/[id]/page.tsx` awaits the board bundle only.
- `repo-card-duplicates.ts`: placements are board placements only. `RepoPlacements` becomes `BoardPlacement[]` (a wrapper object with one key says nothing); `SkippedReason` loses `'maintenance'`; the placement lookup is one query.
- `account/page.tsx`: drop the maintenance count query.
- `board.ts`, `createBoard`: the board and its columns are created in one transaction and returned together. Today the board is inserted first and the columns in a second request; when the second fails, the action reports a failure and leaves a board without columns, and a retry makes a second board. Today it also inserts the name as typed, not the trimmed name its own validation returns; the new call passes the validated name.
- New function `create_board_with_status_lists(p_name text, p_columns jsonb)` in the style of `20260212000000_transaction_rpc_functions.sql` (`LANGUAGE plpgsql`, `SECURITY INVOKER`, fixed `search_path`). Its contract:
  - Input: `p_columns` is a JSON array of `{ "name": text, "color": "#RRGGBB" }`. The array order is the column order: element i, counted from 0, becomes a column with `order` i, `grid_row` 0 and `grid_col` i, the values `createStatusListsFromPreset` writes today. The board page places a column by its two grid fields, so a function that left them at their default of 0 would put every column of a preset into the same cell.
  - Checks, each raising an exception before anything is written: the caller is signed in (`auth.uid()` is not null); the trimmed name has 1 to 50 characters; `p_columns` is an array with at least one element; every element has a name of 1 to 50 characters after trimming and a color of the form `#RRGGBB`. The limits are those of the app's Zod schemas (`BOARD_NAME_MAX_LENGTH`, `STATUS_LIST_NAME_MAX_LENGTH`, `HEX_COLOR_CODE`), named in a comment next to each check. The action stays the place where a user gets a readable validation message; the checks make a direct call to the function as strict as the action. The number of columns has no upper limit: the app has none, and a direct insert into the table has none either.
  - Writes: the board (owner `auth.uid()`, `position` after the user's last board) and its columns in one statement each, under the caller's rights, so RLS applies as it does to a direct insert.
  - Returns `jsonb`: `{ "id", "name", "status_lists": [{ "id", "name", "color" }] }`, the columns in their `order`.
  - Rights: `REVOKE ALL … FROM PUBLIC, anon`, then `GRANT EXECUTE … TO authenticated`.
- `createBoard` checks the returned JSON with a Zod schema, as `getPublicBoardBySlug` does for `get_public_board`, and returns `{ id, name, statusLists }` (id, name and color of each column, the shape the move dialog lists); `/boards/new` keeps reading `id`. A database error is reported to Sentry and returned as "The board was not created. Try again." (an `ActionUserError`); both callers showed "An unexpected error occurred" before. A result that does not match the schema is a defect and not a failed creation, because the board exists: it is reported to Sentry and answered with the generic message, which does not invite a second try.
- `createFirstBoardIfNeeded` (sign-in) creates "First Board" through the same function with the `software-release` columns, so a first board cannot be left without columns either. It still never blocks the sign-in: an error is reported and the user lands on an empty board list. `createDefaultStatusLists` stays for its other caller, the fallback of the board page that gives a board without columns the default ones.
- `board-presets.ts`: new preset `single-column` with one column "Repositories", the name the migrated board uses. `SELECTABLE_PRESET_IDS` holds the six presets `/boards/new` lists; `PRESET_IDS` is those plus `single-column` and stays the list `presetIdSchema` accepts. `PresetSelector` renders `SELECTABLE_PRESET_IDS`. The docstrings of `createBoard`, `BOARD_PRESETS` and the selector say which list is which.

### 3. Client

Removal:

- Move the `BoardOption` type from `RestoreToBoardDialog.tsx` into `MoveToAnotherBoardDialog.tsx`, its remaining user. Then delete `src/app/maintenance/**`, `RestoreToBoardDialog` (+ stories), `DeleteMaintenanceDialog`, `ROUTES.MAINTENANCE`.
- `OverflowMenu`: remove "Move to Maintenance", `context`, `onRestoreToBoard`, `onDelete`, the destructive maintenance item and the generic `TId`; ids are `RepoCardId`.
- Remove the `onMoveToMaintenance`/`onMaintenance` prop chain and `handleMoveToMaintenance`. `useOptimisticCardAction` keeps its remaining caller, Remove from Board; its docstring says so. The engineering review confirmed this: the hook is one unit of optimistic removal, rollback and counted write, and it stays as it is.
- `brands.ts`: delete `MaintenanceId`, `toMaintenanceId`, `CardIdentifier`. `NoteModal`, `useNoteModalDraft` and `draftSlice` use `RepoCardId` instead of a generic id.
- `HeldRepositoryList`: a held repository is always on another board; the `location` union collapses to the board fields.
- `AddRepositoryCombobox`: remove `maintenanceRepoIdentifiers` and the page-load fallback set. When placements are unknown, only repositories on the current board are hidden (the server still refuses duplicates by the unique index, with the message that names the holding board; that copy is unchanged).
- Command Palette: remove "Go to Maintenance" and the `G M - Go to Maintenance` part of the shortcuts hint.
- `/maintenance` returns the app's 404 page. No redirect: nothing links to it and the product has 2 users.

Sidebar:

- Remove the "Maintenance Mode" item and one of the two dividers that framed it. Order after the change, expanded and collapsed: All Boards, Favorites, one divider, Settings, Shortcuts, theme. No link to the migrated board takes the item's place; it is an ordinary board.

Move to Another Board (`MoveToAnotherBoardDialog`), now the only way to set a repository aside with its notes:

| State                                        | What the user sees                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                              |
| -------------------------------------------- | ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| Loading boards                               | unchanged                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                       |
| Loading fails                                | the error text plus a "Try again" button that loads again; never the "no other board" state                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                     |
| No other board                               | "You have no other board yet. Create one to move this repository to." A labeled field "Board name" and a "Create board" button. Creating makes a board with one column "Repositories" (the `single-column` preset of section 2, which `createBoard` accepts and the six-card selector on `/boards/new` does not list), adds the returned board to the dialog's list and selects it and its column. The boards are not loaded again, so a created board is never followed by a failed load. The user then presses Move. A failed creation leaves no board behind (section 2) and shows the action's message under the field (`role="alert"`): the validation text for a name that is empty or too long, otherwise "The board was not created. Try again." The typed name is kept |
| Other boards exist, the user wants a new one | a "New board…" button under the board field swaps the two selects for the same create form, with "Choose an existing board" to go back. After a board is created the selects return with the new board and its column selected                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                  |
| Destination has no column                    | the existing message plus a link "Open <board>". Opening a board without columns gives it the default ones (the fallback in `getBoardBundle`), so the link is all the user needs                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                |
| Move fails                                   | unchanged: inline alert, selections kept                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                        |
| Move succeeds                                | toast "Moved to <board>" with an "Open board" action that goes to that board                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                    |

- Focus: after a board is created, focus goes to the Move button. The field has a visible label, the button reads "Creating…" and is disabled while the request runs, Enter in the field submits. "New board…" moves focus to the name field; "Choose an existing board" moves it back to the board select. The form suggests no name: a default such as "Archive" would bring a special kind of board back through the copy.
- Preselection: the dialog opens on the board the last move from this board went to, when that board is still offered; otherwise on the first board. The memory is one map from source board to destination board in the persisted `settings` slice (this browser only). It keeps parking cheap: once a card went from a working board to the archive board, the next one takes menu, Move to Another Board, Move.
- While the create form is shown no destination is selected: Move is disabled and its handler does nothing. While a creation or a move is running the dialog cannot be closed (Cancel, Escape and a click outside do nothing) and "Choose an existing board" is disabled, so an answer never arrives in a dialog that has moved on.
- The move is a counted board write: the dialog sends `moveCardToBoard` through `trackBoardWrite`, like the other card writes of the board page. When the answer arrives, the card is taken out of the store only if the store still holds the board the card was moved from. Today the handler removes that id from whatever board is on screen, so a user who opened the destination board before the answer arrived loses the card there until the next load.
- Structure: the dialog has 7 `useState` calls and a fetch in an effect today, and this plan adds a mode, a form and a retry. Loading, retry and the list with a created board added go into a hook, `useMoveDestinations`; the create form (name, pending state, error) becomes a component of its own; the dialog body is rendered from one state value with `ts-pattern`, one branch per row of the table above.

Remove from Board (confirmation in `OverflowMenu`):

- The text that says the note, links and comment are deleted gains one sentence: "To keep them, use Move to Another Board instead." Separator and destructive styling stay.

`/account`:

- Two tiles in this order: Boards, Repository Cards. `grid-cols-2` at every width (the three tiles were `grid-cols-3` at every width). `account/loading.tsx` shows two skeleton tiles in the same grid, so the page does not jump from three to two.

Landing `FeaturesSection`:

- Fourth card: icon `ArrowRightLeft`, title "Set Work Aside", text "Move finished or paused repositories to a board of their own. Notes, links and comments stay with each card, so nothing is lost when you bring it back." The card keeps the promise the old one made and says how it works now. Four cards stay: the grid is two columns wide from `md` up.
- The "Project Info" card stops promising "secure credentials"; that feature was removed in `20251231000000_remove_credential_auditlog.sql`. New text: "Add notes and important links to each repository. Keep all project context in one place."
- The four cards lose `cursor-pointer`: they are not links.

### 4. Tests and fixtures

- Delete the 4 `maintenance-*.spec.ts` files and `MaintenanceClient.test.tsx`.
- Remove maintenance cases from the other e2e specs and unit tests; keep every case that tests board behavior. `one-repo-across-boards.spec.ts` and the placement unit tests keep the cross-board cases. A case that used a Maintenance entry only as "some repository held elsewhere" uses a card on another board instead.
- `command-palette.spec.ts` uses "Go to Maintenance" as the unrelated command that a search for "boards" must filter out. It switches to "Go to Settings", so the assertion can still fail.
- `ssr-hydration.spec.ts` lists the landing feature headings; its 'Maintenance Mode' entry becomes the new card's title, so the new card is asserted. `server-rendered-client-components.test.ts` drops its `MaintenanceClient` entry. `account-profile.spec.ts` counts the numeric tiles on `/account` and expects 3; it expects 2.
- `seed.sql`: drop maintenance rows, their `projectinfo`, the `maintenance_id` column from inserts and the header lines that list them. `db-query.ts`: drop maintenance helpers. `mocks/handlers`: drop maintenance handlers and data. Stories: drop maintenance variants.
- New tests, each for behavior that exists after the change. No test asserts that a removed thing is absent: nothing could bring it back by accident, and such a test would itself contain the word the grep forbids.
  - Unit, move dialog: with no other board the create form is shown, a created board becomes the selected destination and Move is enabled, and the boards are not loaded a second time; a failed creation shows its message and keeps the typed name; "New board…" opens the form when other boards exist and "Choose an existing board" closes it; a failed load shows "Try again" and loads again; a destination without a column links to that board; a successful move shows the toast with its "Open board" action; Move is disabled while the create form is shown; Escape during a move leaves the dialog open; the dialog opens on the remembered destination, and on the first board when that one is gone.
  - Unit: `createBoard` with the `single-column` preset sends one column named "Repositories" and returns the created columns; a database failure returns "The board was not created. Try again."; the name is sent trimmed; a result of the wrong shape answers with the generic message; the first sign-in creates "First Board" with the `software-release` columns and a user who has a board gets no second one; a move that is answered after another board was opened leaves that board's cards in place; the preset selector still lists six presets; the Remove from Board confirmation names Move to Another Board; `/account` shows the Boards and Repository Cards tiles and its loading state shows two; the placement split and the 23505 mapping with board placements only.
  - E2E: a card moved to another board and back keeps its note (the park and bring back path); the picker lists a repository of another board under "Already placed elsewhere"; a card is moved to a board created inside the dialog, and that board shows its one column "Repositories" with the card (this run goes through the real function in the local database, which the unit tests mock); a board created on `/boards/new` with a five-column preset shows its five columns in the preset's order, side by side (no spec submits that form today, and it is the test that fails when the function forgets the grid fields).
  - E2E against the function itself, called with the test user's token on the shard's own database: an empty column list is refused; a list whose second column has no name is refused and leaves no board behind (the proof that the board insert is rolled back); a call without a token is refused.
  - New specs use a board name of their own and wait on a visible condition, never on a timeout (`failOnFlakyTests` is on).
  - Migration: no committed test. The rehearsal in section 1 is the check; its counts are recorded in the PR.

### 5. Docs

- `SPEC.md`: remove the Maintenance sections, routes, data model and wireframes; describe archiving as "a board of your own".
- `README.md`, `CLAUDE.md` (schema is 6 tables, route tree, verification rules without Maintenance, the fixture-auth steps that open `/maintenance`, server action list, board data paragraph), `TODOS.md` (delete "Enforce board vs Maintenance exclusivity in the database"; reword the entries that mention Maintenance, restore or the restore dialog; add "Refresh a card's GitHub metadata": `meta` is a snapshot from the moment a card is added and nothing updates it; add "Move focus when a card leaves the board": after Move to Another Board or Remove from Board the focused card is gone and focus falls to the page).
- Retake `public/screenshots/app-screenshot.png` (1200×670, README) and `public/screenshots/social-preview.png` (1280×640). Both show the owner's "Laststance" board in the dark theme with "Maintenance Mode" in the sidebar. Same board and theme, taken from the local copy of production running a production build (`pnpm build` and `pnpm start` with the local Supabase URL and anon key, so no dev indicator is in the frame), with the viewport equal to the image size at device scale factor 1, after the sidebar change; only public repositories in the frame.
- `CLAUDE.md`, "Production Migration Procedure": step 3 tells the reader to link a development project, `jqtxjzdxczqwsrvevmyk`, that no longer exists (`supabase projects list` shows production only, 2026-10-12). It becomes "test locally with `pnpm db:reset`". Below it a short list "Migrations that move or delete data" is added, in general words: count what will be touched; refuse to run when a row cannot be carried over; one transaction with an early return for a second run; dump the data first; compare production's schema with the repository's; rehearse on a copy, with the CLI version the workflow pins (the pin and the local CLI are raised together); compare a manifest before and after. `SPEC.md` names the dead project in three places (environment table, callback table, procedure); they are corrected the same way. The restart command under "Local Supabase + GitHub OAuth Setup" and the line about local development use `pnpm db:stop` and `pnpm db:start`, as the rule above them demands.
- `README.md`, "Getting Started", becomes one ordered path: the pnpm version of `packageManager` (11.1.2; the README says 10.26.2), the GitHub OAuth step (`supabase/.env`) before `pnpm db:start`, which reads that file, and the restart through the scripts. `.env.local.example` starts with `pnpm db:start` instead of bare `supabase start`.
- `TODOS.md`: the existing entry for repository search across boards gains its new reason: the installed PWA has no find-in-page.
- `CHANGELOG.md` and version through `/ship`. The entry says what happened to saved repositories: a board named after the old feature, one card per entry, notes kept. History lives there and in the migration file; the four documents the grep covers describe the product as it is.

### 6. Rollout

The runbook is `docs/plans/2026-10-12-issue-213-rollout-runbook.md`, committed with the PR. It holds, as blocks that can be pasted, every command and SQL statement of the rehearsal (section 1), of the local re-pointing (step 3) and of steps 5, 7 and 10, plus the query that lists guard conflicts. Each block names its input, the expected result and when to stop. It is a document: nothing in it runs in CI or in a test, and no dump, manifest or repository list is committed. The PR body links to it and records the results of this rollout.

1. `pnpm test`, `pnpm lint`, `pnpm build`, `pnpm typecheck`, `pnpm e2e:parallel` (the E2E run resets the local database, so it comes first).
2. Rehearse the migration (section 1, scenarios A and B).
3. Local check as `ryota-murakami` on `http://localhost:3008`, on the database of scenario B. Sign in first (local GoTrue gives the user a new id; the first board of that sign-in is made by the new function and shows its five default columns). While the local user still has only that board: add a repository, open its menu, Move to Another Board, create the destination board in the dialog and finish the move. Then delete the boards of that journey: their cards would collide with the same repositories on the copied boards (one card per user and repository). Then re-point the copied rows from the stub owner id to the local id in one transaction, in this order: `board.user_id`, then `repocard.user_id` (its trigger copies the owner from the board and fires only when the card row itself is updated), then `user_settings` and `user_link_presets` (delete the local user's own row first where the key would collide). The transaction ends with a check that raises when any card's `user_id` differs from the owner of its board. Then, on the copied data, check: the migrated `Maintenance` board and its notes, Move to Another Board in both directions, Remove from Board and its confirmation text, the picker's "Already placed elsewhere" for a repository on the `Maintenance` board, repositories added from the picker to a new board (two columns, one private or organization repository), sidebar (expanded and collapsed), palette, `/account` (also at 320 px wide), the landing page, `/maintenance`. Once with the keyboard only: card menu, move dialog, pick a board, Move. Once at a phone-sized viewport: scroll the `Maintenance` column to a card near its end, open its note, move the card to a working board and back. Then the two screenshots of section 5; before they are committed, each image is looked at once more for a private repository name.
4. `/review`, `/design-review`, `/ship` (draft PR first; CI runs on PRs only). `/design-review` and every check of board creation run on the local copy of production: a preview deployment uses the production database, which gets the new function only at the merge, so creating a board on a preview answers with its error message until then. One PR. Its commits each pass `pnpm typecheck` and keep removal and new behavior apart: (a) removal of the feature from client and server with its tests, stories and fixtures, the move of `shared-project-info.ts` recorded as a rename; (b) the removal migration with `types.ts` and the seed; (c) the function migration with its `types.ts` entry; (d) board creation through it (`createBoard`, the first board at sign-in, the `single-column` preset) and the move dialog with their tests; (e) sidebar divider, `/account` and landing; (f) docs, runbook, the CLI pin of the workflow and screenshots. The exact cut is made while implementing. Two rules hold: a commit that deletes does not also add behavior, and a migration shares its commit with nothing but its types and seed.
5. Right before the merge: run the audit query on production again and note the `entries` count. Expected `entries_also_on_a_board`, `case_variant_groups` and `entries_sharing_an_id_with_a_card`: 0; `projectinfo_policies`: 2. `supabase db push --linked --dry-run` lists the two migrations of this PR and nothing else. Nothing writes to Maintenance between this step and the migration run: the owner is the only user with entries and is away, and no check uses Move to Maintenance or Restore on production. Take a data-only dump of production's `public` schema to a local file outside the repository, and write the manifest of section 1, step 8 from production as JSON next to it (it is also the readable copy of what the table held). The directory is made with `install -d -m 700` and the files are written under `umask 077`, so they are at no moment readable by another account. They hold private repository names and notes; the final report names the files and the day after which the owner can delete them (2026-10-26, two weeks after the rollout). Confirm that the owner has at least two repositories on no board (the scratch boards of step 9 need them). No check on production or on a preview deployment adds a repository that is in Maintenance to a board before the merge (the new picker does not know about Maintenance, and previews use the production database). If the audit shows an overlap anyway, the migration would refuse to run; remove the board card the check added, then merge.
6. Merge. The merge runs `supabase db push` on production at once and Vercel deploys.
7. Wait for the `Supabase Production Migration` run of the merge commit to finish green; its log shows the pinned CLI version and, when the CLI prints it, the migration's `NOTICE`. Then run the verification query on production: table and column gone, one board named `Maintenance`, its card count equal to the count noted in step 5, the `projectinfo` rows on those cards, 2 policies, trigger `O`. Then read the manifest columns back by id and compare them with the manifest of step 5. For the entries and their `projectinfo` rows the comparison is exact: each entry is a card, and each row sits on the card, with the same id, repository, timestamps and content hash; a difference there stops the rollout. For cards that existed before, id, board and repository are the same; another column, `order` or `updated_at` on such a card is a write a user made after the manifest was taken, and it is looked at by hand, not counted as a loss.
8. Wait for the Vercel production deployment of the merge commit.
9. Production check as `ryota-murakami`: board "Maintenance" shows its cards, a known repository is found on it with the browser's find-in-page and its note opens, sidebar/palette/account/landing changed, `/maintenance` is 404, the picker lists a repository of the Maintenance board under "Already placed elsewhere". On two scratch boards: add repositories, Move to Another Board between them, Remove from Board; delete both boards afterwards. The scratch boards need repositories that are on no board; after the migration every former Maintenance repository is placed. If the picker offers none, the add, move and remove flows are reported as not verified on production, with that reason.
10. Fill `meta` of the migrated cards. Maintenance never stored it, so those cards show a bare title next to cards that carry a description, stars and language. For each migrated card read the repository with `gh api` as `ryota-murakami` and collect the fields `addRepositoriesToBoard` stores. Write them in one `DO` block through `supabase db query --linked`: the values travel as one JSON document in a dollar-quoted literal (no quote escaping), one element per card with the card's id, owner, repository and new `meta`. The UPDATE changes a card only when id, owner and repository all match and its `meta` is still empty, and the block raises when the number of rows it changed differs from the number of elements. So a value cannot land on another card, and a second run changes nothing. `update_repocard_updated_at` is disabled before the UPDATE and enabled after it, so the cards keep their `updated_at`. Tried on the local copy first; there the `meta` keys the fill writes are compared with those of a card added through the picker, which shows that the mapping in the runbook is the app's. By hand: the commands are in the runbook, the values are not committed. The step starts by confirming that `gh` acts as `ryota-murakami`. GitHub's answers fall into three groups: returned (filled); 404 (the repository is gone: the card keeps its empty `meta` and is listed in the report); anything else, such as a rate limit, 401 or 5xx (nothing is written for that card, the fill is reported as not complete and the step is run again later). Check afterwards, by id and not by count: the `meta` of every migrated card, read back from production, equals the document that was sent; the manifest columns of all cards are read once more and show no change, `updated_at` included; the trigger is enabled; the board in the browser shows descriptions.

When the migration run fails, the log says which of three cases it is. On `lock_timeout` or a deadlock (`40P01`, "deadlock detected"; the two functions this migration drops lock the same tables in the opposite order, and a call of one of them may be running): nothing is wrong with the migration, so run it again with `gh workflow run supabase-production.yml` (the workflow has `workflow_dispatch`). On a refusal (the message starts with `remove_maintenance_mode:`): the migration is right and the data or the schema is not what it expects; list the conflicts with the runbook query, resolve them as the `DETAIL` says and run the workflow again, without a commit. On any other error the migration has a defect: the database is unchanged (one transaction) and the failed migration is not recorded, so the fix is an edit of the same migration file in a new commit on `main`, which triggers the run again. Meanwhile the new build runs on a schema that still has the table; the table of builds and schemas below says what works then. The Maintenance entries are intact and not reachable from the UI until the migration succeeds.

When the migration succeeds and the result is wrong: rebuild the affected cards by hand from the rows in the dump of step 5 (its `maintenance` inserts target a table that no longer exists, so the file is a source of values, not something to replay). The manifest comparison of step 7 names the rows that are missing or different. Point-in-time recovery is the last resort; it also rolls back every other write made since.

Known window: in earlier runs the migration finished about 35 s after the merge (estimate), and the Vercel build takes a few minutes. Until the new build is live, the old code runs on the new schema. The board page keeps working (`getUserMaintenanceRepoIdentifiers` ignores the query error and returns an empty list), and so do notes, links and comments of board cards (the old code reads and writes them by `repo_card_id` only, `shared-project-info.ts:236` and `:299`). The old Maintenance page shows an empty list, Move to Maintenance fails with an error toast, the picker's placement lookup shows its error state, and adding a repository is refused by the server too (`lookupRepoPlacements` throws when its Maintenance query fails). A tab opened before the deploy keeps the old client; its server actions belong to the old build, and a reload brings the new one. Accepted (2 users, night rollout); the alternative is two PRs.

Builds and schemas. The rollout passes through three schema states: A, before both migrations; B, the function exists and the table is still there (the removal refused or failed); C, after both.

|           | Schema A                                                                                                                                                               | Schema B                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                           | Schema C                                                                                                                                                            |
| --------- | ---------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | ---------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| Old build | today                                                                                                                                                                  | as today                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                           | the known window above: boards, cards, notes and board creation work; the Maintenance page is empty; Move to Maintenance and adding a repository fail with an error |
| New build | reached only when the function migration failed: everything works except board creation, which answers "The board was not created. Try again." until the fix is pushed | everything works and the entries are shown nowhere. A repository that is in Maintenance can be added to a board, and that card would make the removal refuse. Only the owner has entries and the owner is away; whoever runs the rollout stays with it until the migration is green, and resolves a refusal with the runbook query | the goal                                                                                                                                                            |

## Out of scope

- A board preset or a special board type for archiving. A board is a board.
- Renaming or reordering the migrated board for the owner; they can rename it. It is not marked as a favorite either: a migration does not set a preference, and the handoff note of the final report links to the board.
- Putting a moved card at the top of its new column. Every card enters a column at the end; the success toast opens the board.
- An undo for Move to Another Board, and starting the dialog without a preselected board. Moving back goes through the same dialog and loses nothing.
- Resolving an entry whose repository is also on a board, or entries that differ only in letter case. The migration refuses to run instead; production has neither.
- An archived flag on cards or boards. It would keep a second state that every board query, the picker and the counts have to filter; the issue asks for a plain board.
- A "refresh from GitHub" action for card metadata. It is a feature of its own (about ten files with its tests); it goes to TODOS.md. The cards this migration creates get their `meta` once, by hand (section 6, step 10).
- Editing old migrations or `docs/plans/2026-10-11-*.md` (history).
- `.github/release.yml` "🔧 Maintenance" release-notes category (unrelated meaning).
- A reverse migration. It would have to bring back the table, the column, the policies and the functions for code that no longer exists. A wrong result is repaired forward from the manifest and the dump.
- Measuring first-time setup from a fresh clone. The README is put in order (section 5); the time is bounded by Docker image downloads and registering an OAuth app, and this change does not alter that path.
- `CHECK` constraints for name length and color on `board` and `statuslist`. The new function checks its own input; a direct write to those tables is limited by RLS to the caller's own rows, as before.
- A committed test of the removal migration, and a reverse of the metadata fill. The first is replaced by the rehearsal and the manifest; the second is one UPDATE that sets `meta` back to `'{}'` for the ids of the manifest.
