## What the implementation did differently

Written after the build. The plan above is kept as it was approved.

- `BoardOption` lives in `src/lib/actions/types.ts`, not in the dialog file. Two server actions and three client files use it.
- A refusal of the removal migration carries its next step in `DETAIL`, not in `HINT`. Supabase CLI 2.120.0 prints the message and the `DETAIL` of a failed migration and nothing else, so a `HINT` would never be read. `NOTICE` lines are not printed either, and no step depends on them.
- The removal migration has two guards the plan did not ask for. It refuses to run in a session that row level security applies to: such a session would count no entries and then drop the table with its data. And it checks, before anything is dropped, that every entry has become a card with the same id, owner and repository and that no note still points at an entry.
- The dialog owns the flag "a board is being created", because it also keeps itself open for that time. The form owns the name and its error text.
- "Move focus when a card leaves the board" was built, not deferred (design decision 6B said TODO). `useCardDialogFocusReturn` puts the focus on the next card of the column after Move to Another Board and Remove from Board, and back on the card after Cancel. `TODOS.md` therefore gets one new entry, not two.
- `lastVisitedBoard` left the board slice. The page this change removes was its only reader.
- The shard databases of `pnpm e2e:parallel` now grant `USAGE` on schema `auth` to `anon`, `authenticated` and `service_role`, as Supabase does. The shards are built from a dump without privileges, and `create_board_with_status_lists` runs with the caller's rights, so its `auth.uid()` was refused there with "permission denied for schema auth". Production and the main local database had the grant all along.
- The move dialog lists boards as before, newest first. The board the migration creates is the newest one, so a first move from any board is offered it. A board created inside the dialog is put first in its list for the same reason: that is where the next opening shows it.
- Comments and test samples no longer name the two deleted Supabase projects; the fallback URL of the MSW handlers is the local Supabase.

### Changed by the code review

The pre-landing review (eight specialist readers, a red team, two adversarial readers and a structured review by a second model) ran on the nine commits of the build. What it changed:

- `createFirstBoardIfNeeded`, `createBoardFromPreset` and `createDefaultStatusLists` moved to `src/lib/actions/board-creation.ts`, a file without `'use server'`. In `board.ts` they were endpoints any signed-in browser could call. `createFirstBoardIfNeeded` counted the boards of a user id it was handed, row level security answers 0 for a foreign id, and the new database function takes the owner from the session: a caller who sent another id got one more "First Board" each time, outside the rate limit. The callback now hands over its own client and the id of the session it just established.
- `createBoardFromPreset` has a fourth outcome, `unconfirmed`. An error without a code did not come from the database (a lost request, a gateway), so the board may exist, and the user reads "Could not confirm that the board was created. Check your boards before trying again." where the plan's "The board was not created. Try again." would have invited a second board.
- Both migrations add the new board's position as `bigint` and cap it at 2147483647. A board at that position, which the position validator allows, would have made `MAX(position) + 1` overflow: the removal would have been rolled back for every user.
- `create_board_with_status_lists` takes at most 100 columns and asks the length of an array only (`CASE`, not `OR`).
- A third migration, `20261012085950_statuslist_color_check.sql`: a column color is `#RRGGBB` or `NULL`. The color is printed into a style attribute, on a shared board into the page a visitor loads, and the table accepted any text from a direct API write. Not part of issue #213; found while reading the function that validates the same value. It sorts before the removal and gives a column that holds another value the default color: it cannot refuse for a reason in the data, and it does not wait for a removal that can. The shared page reads the color through the same check (`publicBoardColumnSchema`), so a value that is not `#RRGGBB` is shown as the default even if a row carried one.
- A conflict of the removal is resolved by deleting the entry, never a card: a card that has the id of an entry can belong to another account, since any signed-in user can write an entry with an id of their choice. The refusal says so, and section 7 of the runbook has the statements that list the conflicts (with `same_owner`), print the note of an entry and delete an entry.
- The metadata fill (section 8 of the runbook) reads GitHub for the entries of the token's own account only, found through the GitHub id the token reports, and only for names GitHub can have. It ran `gh api repos/<owner>/<name>` with the owner's token for every entry, and an entry of another user naming a private repository of the owner would have copied its description onto that user's card.
- The move dialog lists the columns of a destination in the order its board shows them (`grid_row`, `grid_col`). It ordered by `order`, which a column drag never changes, so the default column could differ from the left-most one on screen.
- The dialog waits 15 seconds for a move or a board creation (`settleWithin`), then reports an unknown outcome and can be closed again. Design decision "the dialog cannot be closed while a request runs" stays; it gained an end. Every move that was not confirmed has the board fetched again, and `moveCardToBoard` lost its look into the target board for the same repository: with one card per user and repository that look could only find the card itself, and it turned the second try of a move whose answer was lost into "Repository already exists in target board". Its answer for a unique violation went the same way (a card stays with its owner, so a move cannot cause one), together with the sentence "… was just placed on a board".
- `trackBoardWrite` counts a save as pending for the same 15 seconds at most (`SERVER_ANSWER_TIME_LIMIT_MS`). A request that never got an answer kept the count above zero for good, and with it every later fetch of the board, the one the dialog asks for included. A save that is answered after the limit asks for one more fetch.
- In the dialog: the board and column selects are disabled while a request runs, the button for a new board is an outline button with a 44px touch target, the link to a board without columns is underlined foreground text, the "Moved to" toast stays 8 seconds, a board created in the dialog is announced to screen readers, and the `isOpen` prop is gone (the page mounts the dialog per opening).
- The copy of the create form follows `/boards/new`: "Board Name", "Create Board", "Creating...", and "New Board..." for the button that opens it (the plan wrote "Board name", "Create board", "Creating…", "New board…"). The field counts its characters ("7/50 characters") as that page does.
- After a creation that could not be confirmed (no answer, a broken connection, an answer that cannot be read), the form offers "Reload Boards" in place of "Create Board" and the name is not sent a second time: the board may exist, and the list on screen would not show it. `/boards/new` words the same case as "Board creation not confirmed" and links to the boards. A code that says the connection broke (`08…`, `57P…`, `PGRST00…`) counts as "no answer", and an unreadable answer gets the same text in place of "An unexpected error occurred".
- Keyboard focus inside the dialog: every control is disabled while a request runs, which drops the focus to the page. Each ending puts it on the control needed next (Move, the name field, "Reload Boards"), the Board select takes it once the boards are loaded (the dialog opens on Cancel while they load), and the close button in the corner is disabled while the dialog refuses to close.
- After a move without a confirmation the board is fetched again, and the card can be gone from it while the dialog is still open (the move went through all the same). Closing the dialog then left the focus on the page itself. The dialog now picks the card's neighbor before that fetch (`rememberCardMayLeave`), and the neighbor takes the focus when the card is gone at closing time; a card that is still there takes it as before. Found in the local check with the real account, by holding a move for 17 seconds.
- What the dialog holds for one opening (the destination picked so far, the create form, the request it waits for, the reason of a move that did not go through) moved into a hook, `useMoveDialogState`. It hands the dialog one view and named steps (`pickBoard`, `startMove`, `failMove`, `selectCreatedBoard`, ...), and the component keeps the requests, the keyboard focus and the markup. The rule of this repository extracts a hook at three `useState` calls, and the component had four.
- `Z` no longer undoes a drag while a dialog, a menu or a list of options is open. A Radix Select reads a typed letter as "jump to this option" and lets the key travel on to the window, so typing "z" on the Board select of the move dialog undid the last drag on the board behind it.
- The label of a destructive button is black in the seven dark themes (`--destructive-foreground`), and the Remove confirmation uses the token. Near-white read 2.8:1 on the default dark theme. The reds themselves moved too: `oklch(0.5 0.2 27)` in the light themes and `oklch(0.64 0.2 22)` in the six tinted dark ones. With the reds before, at least one of three readings was below 4.5:1 in every token set but the two of the default dark theme, down to 3.5:1: the label on the hovered fill, red text on the page (the menu item "Remove from Board", an error text) and red text on its own 10% tint. A unit test now computes all three for every theme.
- `src/lib/actions/auth-guard.ts` is no longer a `'use server'` file. Its three wrappers were endpoints that took a rate limit key, and a Server Action to run, from the caller. A unit test lists every endpoint of `src/lib/actions` and fails for an export it does not list.
- Tests the review asked for: the move as a counted board write, requests that reject or never answer, the rights and limits of the function against a real database, row level security of `projectinfo` after its policies were recreated, the color check, the focus attributes on the components that emit them and the focus hand-over when a column empties, the presets against the function's limits, the OAuth callback handing its own client and user to the first-board creation, the failure branches of that creation, the default columns of a board found without any, the ownership refusals of a move, and the two workflows pinning one CLI version.
- The story test "Optimistic Favorite Toggle" (`BoardGrid.stories.tsx`) failed in the last round of checks. It is not part of this change: on `main` it failed 1 run of 12, on this branch 5 of 24. The `vi.mock` of `src/lib/actions/board.ts` in `.storybook/vitest.setup.ts` is put in place while the tests run and sometimes lost against the story file's own import; then the real action ran, answered "Authentication required" within 2 ms and the card took the star back. The module is now registered with `sb.mock` in `.storybook/preview.tsx`, which rewrites it while it is transformed, and the two story files that press the star say what the server answers. 30 runs in a row passed afterwards.
- `.github/workflows/e2e.yml` pins the Supabase CLI to 2.120.0, the version of the production workflow. The shard script repeats the `REVOKE` and `GRANT` of the function, so a shard refuses a visitor for the same reason production does.

The third pass of the review read the fixes of the first two and changed these:

- The metadata fill of the runbook (section 8) writes only to cards on a board of the account the data was read for (`v_owner`). The document it sends is built from the owner's entries, but the statement itself matched by id, repository and an empty `meta` alone. Its local trial runs before the hand-over of section 4, because the hand-over gives the cards to another account.
- The blocks of sections 5, 6 and 8 of the runbook define what they need. Run in a new shell they found no `STAMP`, compared two files that did not exist and printed nothing, which reads like a clean result; the fill sent an empty document and its check passed. They stop now, and the audit of section 5 reads every condition the removal checks itself (both policy names, row level security on all five tables, the owners of the two functions).
- `/boards/new` ends its form after a creation that could not be confirmed: "Open your boards" takes the place of "Create Board" and the name is not sent a second time. It only worded the case before. A request that fails on the way or gets no answer in 15 seconds counts as unconfirmed too; it used to reach the error page of the route, which lost the name and offered a second try. The form and the move dialog share that logic in `requestBoardCreation`.
- `createBoard` marks an unconfirmed creation with `errorCode: 'BOARD_CREATION_UNCONFIRMED'`. The browser compared the error text with a constant.
- Only a SQLSTATE or a PostgREST code proves that the database refused a creation. A gateway's own JSON can carry any `code`, a number included, and a number made the check throw: inside the OAuth callback that sent a signed-in user back to the login page. `createFirstBoardIfNeeded` cannot throw any more.
- A drag, an undo or a removal that gets no answer in 15 seconds has the board fetched again and says so ("Could not confirm that the change was saved"), with a warning in Sentry. `trackBoardWrite` only stopped counting such a save, so the screen kept a change nobody had confirmed without a word. An overdue move or creation leaves a warning in Sentry as well.
- Next.js sends Server Actions and `router.refresh()` one after another, so the fetch after an overdue request waits until that request has settled. The notices of an unknown outcome (the toast of a save, the message of a move) offer "Reload page", which waits for nothing, and the limit is documented with that queue.
- The move dialog marks a board that is shared by a public link, says what the link shows of the repository when that board is the destination, and does not open on a shared board unless the last move went there or no other board exists. One press of Move on the newest board could put the name and the description of a private repository on a public page. The Add Repositories picker says the same on a shared board.
- The dialog keeps the name of the repository it was opened for. The board page read it from the cards on the board, so the name went empty in the open dialog, and in the "Moved to" toast of a second try, once a move without a confirmation had taken the card off the board.
- The dialog picks the neighbor of the card when the move is sent, not when its failure is known: an overdue move has the board fetched again first.
- "Delete Column", "Delete" on a board card and "Delete" on a comment are highlighted with a tint of their own red (`focus:bg-destructive/10`), as "Remove from Board" is. Red text on the accent color read about 2:1 in six dark themes. A unit test reads the class names of every component and fails for a destructive color used outside the pairs the contrast test computes.
- `state.board.activeBoard` is gone. Nothing read it since `lastVisitedBoard` left with this change.
- `Z` tests the key before it asks whether an overlay is open, the skeleton of `/account` has the width of the page, and the name field of `/boards/new` announces a refused name and is tied to its hint.
- Tests the third pass asked for: odd arguments of the board function (`null`, a text, a column without a color, exactly 100 columns), the position of a new board, a move sent twice against the database, the rules of `projectinfo` (no row without a card, one row per card, gone with its card), a board whose last column was deleted, and a shared destination in a browser.

Advice of the review that was not taken, with the reason:

- Narrowing `useOptimisticCardAction` to its one caller and folding `project-info-core.ts` into `project-info.ts`: decisions 66 and 21 of the review record keep both as they are.
- Shipping the code first and the removal migration in a second push: migrating first is what keeps the build before this PR from adding a repository that is in Maintenance to a board, and one PR was asked for. Section 9 of the runbook has the case "migration green, deployment failed".
- Enforcing in code that a preview of the new build cannot add a Maintenance repository to a board: the audit of section 5 runs right before the merge, and the migration refuses without changing anything.
- A CI job that runs the removal with data: the migration runs once; sections 2 and 3 of the runbook are its rehearsal.
- Dropping `reason`, `boardId` and `boardName` from a skipped repository of the picker: they are the answer shape of issue #215, and this change only took one value out of `reason`.
- A wording of its own for "the API does not know the function yet" (PGRST202): it can only happen in the minutes after the migration, "Try again" is right for it, and section 9 of the runbook has the case.
- Recovering from an overdue request by a navigation, which goes ahead of the queue of Next.js: "Reload page" does the same with nothing new to get wrong, and TODOS.md keeps the step that removes the queue from the path (fetching the board through a way of its own).
- Dropping the URL props of the card menu that no caller sets, `CSS.escape` in the lookup of a card element, and the two parameters of `toUniqueViolationError` that are constant now: none of them is part of this change, and each works as it is.
- Asking for a confirmation before a private repository is moved to a shared board: the dialog says what the link shows and never picks such a board by itself. Whether a public page should show a private repository at all is a product decision; TODOS.md has it.

## Review record

### Phase 0: intake

- Input: GitHub issue #213 ("Maintainnaiceモードの廃止", opened 2026-10-10, body: a Maintenance Mode board does the same, so abolish it). No plan file existed; this file was written from the issue and a read of the code, then handed to `/autoplan`.
- Owner is away for the whole run (message of 2026-10-12: no replies possible; done means deployed and checked on production as `ryota-murakami`). Every gate in this run is therefore decided by the 6 principles and the owner's written direction at the top of the plan. `/office-hours` offer: skipped for the same reason. gstack upgrade prompt (1.91.86.0 → 1.91.89.0): not taken mid-run, reported at the end.
- Base branch `main`, platform GitHub, repo mode solo, no open PRs, no design doc.
- UI scope: yes (page, dialogs, menu, sidebar, palette, account tiles, landing card). DX scope: yes by the tool's term count (`CLI`, `command` ×3, `import`, `action`; `dxRequiredByTerms: true`, input sha `2d4c446c…`). The matches are "Command Palette" and "server action", not a developer-facing surface, but the rule allows no override of a positive result, so Phase 2.5 runs.
- Outside reviewer preflight: `CODEX_MODE: ready`.

### Phase 1: CEO review (mode: SELECTIVE EXPANSION, /autoplan override)

Methodology read: `autoplan-ceo-methodology-5ichr0/methodology.md`, ranges 1-600, 601-1200, 1201-1800, 1801-2400, 2401-2503 of 2503 lines (through EOF).

#### System audit

- `main` is clean and equal to `origin/main` (334597c). Three `lint-staged` stashes, unrelated. No in-flight branch touches Maintenance.
- Most-touched files in 30 days: `CLAUDE.md`, `TODOS.md`, `SPEC.md`, `AddRepositoryCombobox.tsx`, `BoardPageClient.tsx`. Two of them are in this plan's path; both were last changed by #215 and #218, which are merged.
- Retrospective: #215 (2026-10-11) added Maintenance awareness in five places (placement lookup, skip reason, held rows, restore duplicate check, TODO for DB exclusivity). This plan deletes all five. The recurring cost of the parallel path is the architectural finding that motivates the issue.
- TODOS.md entries this plan touches: "Enforce board vs Maintenance exclusivity in the database" (moot), "Open-board links inside the move and restore dialog errors" and "Run the independent queries of add and restore in parallel" (restore half moot), "Clear the persisted board data on sign-out" and "Word every failed board save from what the rollback did" (mention Maintenance in their reasoning).
- Taste calibration. Good references: `supabase/migrations/20261011011500_repocard_unique_repo_per_user.sql` (one `DO` block, counts-only messages, re-runnable) and `src/lib/actions/repo-card-duplicates.ts` (pure helpers outside the `'use server'` file). Pattern to avoid: `FkConfig` in `shared-project-info.ts`, an abstraction over two owners that this plan reduces to one.
- Landscape (WebSearch, Aside not installed): [GitHub Projects](https://github.blog/2018-06-28-archive-project-board-cards) archives to a separate view that search does not cover; [Kanban Zone](https://help.kanbanzone.com/en/articles/11960-archiving-and-unarchiving-a-card) keeps an "Archived" column on the board. Layer 1: both shapes ship in real tools. Layer 2: the separate view hides items from search; the column clutters the board. Layer 3: GitBox cards are repositories, not tasks, and the product already has many boards per user, so a third shape exists at zero code: an archive board. For a 2-user tool the second storage path costs more than it returns. No eureka to log; this is the issue's own reasoning.
- Prior learnings applied: `prod-migration-no-approval-gate` (10/10, 2026-10-10), `gitbox-ci-runs-on-pr-only-draft-first` (9/10, 2026-10-11), `pr-body-closing-keyword` (10/10, 2026-10-10), `next-build-loads-env-production` (9/10, 2026-10-11).

#### 0A. Premise challenge

Real problem: two storage paths for one thing. Target outcome: one path, archive kept. Do-nothing cost: every board feature keeps paying a Maintenance tax (five touch points in #215 alone). The plan removes the cause, not a proxy.

| #   | Premise                                | Claim                                                               | Receipt                                                                                                                                            | Verdict                                            |
| --- | -------------------------------------- | ------------------------------------------------------------------- | -------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | -------------------------------------------------- |
| 1   | Parallel storage                       | `maintenance` is its own table with its own uniqueness rule         | `supabase/migrations/20251115_initial_schema.sql:80`                                                                                               | verified                                           |
| 2   | Two owners of `projectinfo`            | exclusive CHECK between `repo_card_id` and `maintenance_id`         | `supabase/migrations/20260101170000_add_maintenance_id_to_projectinfo.sql:19`                                                                      | verified                                           |
| 3   | A board does the same job              | a card moves between boards and keeps its note, links and comment   | `supabase/migrations/20260220035156_add_move_card_to_board_rpc.sql:4`, `src/lib/actions/repo-cards.ts:504`                                         | verified, with gaps (below)                        |
| 4   | Data volume                            | 23 rows, 1 user, 2 `projectinfo`, 0 overlap, 0 boards named like it | [measured: `supabase db query --linked`, production, 2026-10-12]                                                                                   | verified                                           |
| 5   | Board names are free                   | no unique constraint on `board.name`                                | `supabase/migrations/20251115_initial_schema.sql:19`                                                                                               | verified                                           |
| 6   | `FkConfig` serves two owners only      | two call sites, one per owner                                       | `src/lib/actions/shared-project-info.ts:44`                                                                                                        | verified                                           |

| 8   | Nothing else links to `/maintenance`   | Sidebar, Command Palette, held rows; not the manifest               | `src/components/Sidebar/Sidebar.tsx:320`, `src/components/CommandPalette/CommandPalette.tsx:92`, `src/components/Board/HeldRepositoryList.tsx:116` | verified                                           |
| 9   | Migration runs on merge                | `supabase db push --linked` on push to `main` touching migrations   | `.github/workflows/supabase-production.yml:46`                                                                                                     | verified                                           |

Gaps in premise 3, accepted: the Maintenance page has search, sort and a list view; a board column has none. 23 cards in one column is a long scroll. The owner asked for this trade in the issue, columns can be added and cards dragged, and Compact Mode exists. Repo search across boards is already in TODOS.md ("Repo search in the command palette").

#### 0B. Existing code leverage

Nothing is rebuilt. The only new code is the migration and a few tests.

#### 0C. Dream state

```
CURRENT STATE                    THIS PLAN                         12-MONTH IDEAL
two places for a repo      --->  one place: a card on a board --->  one place, cards keyed by
(board card or Maintenance       archive = a board the user         GitHub repo id, repo search
row), two owners of              names; projectinfo has one         across boards, and every
projectinfo, app-only            owner; repo_card_id NOT NULL;      placement rule in the
exclusivity between them         exclusivity is a DB fact           database
```

The plan moves straight toward the ideal: it removes the one placement rule that could not be expressed in the database.

#### 0D. Approach

No required choice. The issue names the approach (delete the feature, use a board). The one open sub-choice, drop the data or move it, is settled by P1 (completeness): move it. The owner allows loss; the plan does not use the allowance.

#### 0E. Mode

SELECTIVE EXPANSION, set by `/autoplan`. By file count alone (107 tracked files mention Maintenance) the skill's rule would point at SCOPE REDUCTION; the count is inherent to a removal and most of it is deletion. Handoff line sent in chat: "Mode: SELECTIVE EXPANSION; approved decisions: none." Provenance: `/autoplan` override, no question asked.

#### 0F / 0G. Scope proposals

HOLD-scope checks first. Complexity: no new class or service; one migration; the rest is deletion and narrowing of types. Minimum change for the goal would leave the generic `TId`, `FkConfig` and the wrapper type in place; the owner asked for the aggressive version, and each of them is an abstraction with one remaining user, so they go. Nothing is deferrable without leaving a dead abstraction behind.

10x view: the removal is the leverage. After it, any board feature (search, bulk move, sharing) serves archived repos for free.

<!-- autoplan-baseline-edits:ceo {"sourceSha256":"2d4c446c10bccc6abe26dcf8898f2719e8ad8b8d9a2770a3b05fcf2a7d223367","replacements":[{"oldText":"## Goal\n\nGitBox has one place for a repository: a card on a board. The Maintenance page, table, RPCs, actions, UI entries, tests and docs are gone. Nothing the owner archived is lost: every Maintenance entry becomes a card on a board named \"Maintenance\".\n\nSuccess:\n\n- `git grep -i maintenance -- src e2e mocks supabase/seed.sql` returns only unrelated uses (none expected).\n- Production after rollout: table `maintenance` and column `projectinfo.maintenance_id` do not exist; the owner has a board \"Maintenance\" holding 23 cards; the 2 notes that lived on Maintenance entries open from their cards.\n\n","newText":"## Goal\n\nGitBox has one place for a repository: a card on a board. The Maintenance page, table, RPCs, actions, UI entries, tests and docs are gone. The owner's archive is kept: every Maintenance entry becomes a card on a new board named \"Maintenance\". The migration refuses to run while an entry cannot become a card (its repository is already on a board, or two entries differ only in letter case); production has none (audit below).\n\nWhat the Maintenance page had and a board does not: search by name, sorting and a list view. The issue accepts that trade. The browser's find-in-page reaches every card on a board, and repository search across boards is already in TODOS.md.\n\nSuccess:\n\n- `git grep -i maintenance -- src e2e mocks supabase/seed.sql eslint.config.mjs knip.config.ts README.md CLAUDE.md SPEC.md TODOS.md` returns no lines.\n- `public/screenshots/app-screenshot.png` and `social-preview.png` no longer show a \"Maintenance Mode\" sidebar entry.\n- On ordinary boards a repository can be parked, found again and brought back with its note: on the local copy of production, find a card on the \"Maintenance\" board, open its note, move it to a working board and back. A user with one board is led from the move dialog to creating a second one.\n- Production after rollout: table `maintenance` and column `projectinfo.maintenance_id` do not exist; the owner has a board \"Maintenance\" whose card count equals the `maintenance` row count taken right before the merge (23 on 2026-10-12); the `projectinfo` rows that lived on Maintenance entries (2 on 2026-10-12) open from their cards; the migrated cards whose repository GitHub still returns show a description.\n\n"},{"oldText":"## Current state\n\nAudit on production, counts only (2026-10-12): 23 `maintenance` rows, all of 1 user; 2 `projectinfo` rows linked through `maintenance_id`; 0 repositories both on a board and in Maintenance; 0 case-variant duplicates; 0 boards whose name contains \"maintenance\"; 5 boards, 27 cards, 2 users.\n\nWhere Maintenance lives today:\n\n- DB: table `maintenance` (`unique_repo_per_user`), `projectinfo.maintenance_id` (FK, unique, exclusive CHECK with `repo_card_id`, which is nullable because of it), RLS policies on `projectinfo` with a maintenance path, functions `move_to_maintenance` and `restore_to_board`.\n- Server: `moveToMaintenance`, `restoreToBoard` (`src/lib/actions/repo-cards.ts`), `src/lib/actions/maintenance-project-info.ts`, `getUserMaintenanceRepoIdentifiers` and `BoardPageData.maintenanceRepoIdentifiers` (`board-data.ts`), maintenance placement lookup and the `'maintenance'` skip reason (`repo-card-duplicates.ts`), `FkConfig` in `shared-project-info.ts` (exists only to serve two owners), maintenance count on `/account`.\n- Client: route `src/app/maintenance/**` (page, layout, loading, error, client, 4 hooks, stories), `RestoreToBoardDialog`, `DeleteMaintenanceDialog`, \"Move to Maintenance\" in `OverflowMenu` plus its `context`/`onRestoreToBoard`/`onDelete` props and generic `TId`, `onMaintenance` prop chain (`BoardPageClient` → `KanbanBoard` → `SortableColumn`/`StatusColumn` → `RepoCard`), \"In Maintenance\" rows in `HeldRepositoryList`, maintenance fallback in `AddRepositoryCombobox`, Sidebar entry, Command Palette entry and `G M` shortcut text, `ROUTES.MAINTENANCE`, `MaintenanceId`/`toMaintenanceId`/`CardIdentifier` brands, generic `TId` in `NoteModal`, landing \"Maintenance Mode\" feature card, \"Maintenance Items\" tile on `/account`.\n- Tests and fixtures: 4 `e2e/logged-in/maintenance-*.spec.ts`, maintenance cases in 9 other specs, `MaintenanceClient.test.tsx`, maintenance cases in about 14 unit test files, stories, `mocks/handlers/{data,supabase}.ts`, `supabase/seed.sql`, `e2e/helpers/db-query.ts`, `eslint.config.mjs` entry.\n- Docs: `SPEC.md`, `README.md`, `CLAUDE.md`, `TODOS.md`.\n\n","newText":"## Current state\n\nAudit on production, counts only (2026-10-12): 23 `maintenance` rows, all of 1 user; 2 `projectinfo` rows linked through `maintenance_id`; 0 repositories both on a board and in Maintenance; 0 case-variant duplicates; 0 boards whose name contains \"maintenance\"; 5 boards, 27 cards, 2 users.\n\nThe audit query (the same comparison the migration uses; run again in section 6, step 5):\n\n```sql\nSELECT\n  (SELECT count(*) FROM maintenance) AS entries,\n  (SELECT count(DISTINCT user_id) FROM maintenance) AS users_with_entries,\n  (SELECT count(*) FROM projectinfo WHERE maintenance_id IS NOT NULL) AS projectinfo_on_entries,\n  (SELECT count(*) FROM maintenance m JOIN repocard r ON r.user_id = m.user_id\n     AND lower(r.repo_owner) = lower(m.repo_owner)\n     AND lower(r.repo_name) = lower(m.repo_name)) AS entries_also_on_a_board,\n  (SELECT count(*) FROM (SELECT 1 FROM maintenance\n     GROUP BY user_id, lower(repo_owner), lower(repo_name)\n     HAVING count(*) > 1) d) AS case_variant_groups;\n```\n\nWhere Maintenance lives today:\n\n- DB: table `maintenance` (`unique_repo_per_user`, case-sensitive), `projectinfo.maintenance_id` (FK, unique, exclusive CHECK with `repo_card_id`, which is nullable because of it), two RLS policies on `projectinfo` with a maintenance path, functions `move_to_maintenance` and `restore_to_board`.\n- Server: `moveToMaintenance`, `restoreToBoard` (`src/lib/actions/repo-cards.ts`), `src/lib/actions/maintenance-project-info.ts`, `getUserMaintenanceRepoIdentifiers` and `BoardInitialData.maintenanceRepoIdentifiers` (`board-data.ts`), maintenance placement lookup and the `'maintenance'` skip reason (`repo-card-duplicates.ts`), `FkConfig` in `shared-project-info.ts` (exists only to serve two owners), maintenance count on `/account`.\n- Client: route `src/app/maintenance/**` (page, layout, loading, error, client, 4 hooks, stories), `RestoreToBoardDialog`, `DeleteMaintenanceDialog`, \"Move to Maintenance\" in `OverflowMenu` plus its `context`/`onRestoreToBoard`/`onDelete` props and generic `TId`, `onMaintenance` prop chain (`BoardPageClient` → `KanbanBoard` → `SortableColumn`/`StatusColumn` → `RepoCard`), \"In Maintenance\" rows in `HeldRepositoryList`, maintenance fallback in `AddRepositoryCombobox`, Sidebar entry, Command Palette entry and its `G M` hint, `ROUTES.MAINTENANCE`, `MaintenanceId`/`toMaintenanceId`/`CardIdentifier` brands and their users (`NoteModal`, `useNoteModalDraft`, `draftSlice`), landing \"Maintenance Mode\" feature card, \"Maintenance Items\" tile on `/account`.\n- Tests and fixtures: 4 `e2e/logged-in/maintenance-*.spec.ts`, maintenance cases in 9 other specs, `MaintenanceClient.test.tsx`, maintenance cases in 16 other unit test files, 11 story files, `mocks/handlers/{data,supabase}.ts`, `supabase/seed.sql`, `e2e/helpers/db-query.ts`, `eslint.config.mjs` and `knip.config.ts` entries.\n- Docs: `SPEC.md`, `README.md`, `CLAUDE.md`, `TODOS.md`.\n\nThe lists above name the known places. The definition of done is the `git grep` in Success: comments, docstrings, test names and fixture headers that mention Maintenance are changed too.\n\n"},{"oldText":"### 1. Migration `<ts>_remove_maintenance_mode.sql`\n\nOne `DO` block (atomic however the CLI wraps the file), re-runnable, `lock_timeout` 5s, `ACCESS EXCLUSIVE` locks on `maintenance`, `projectinfo`, `repocard` taken first. Messages carry counts only.\n\n1. Move data. For each user that has `maintenance` rows:\n   - Create a board named `Maintenance` at the end of the user's board order (`position = max + 1`), with one column `Archived` (default gray, `order` 0, grid 0/0). A user who already has a board with that name still gets a new one; board names are not unique.\n   - Insert one `repocard` per maintenance row whose repository is on none of the user's boards. The card reuses the maintenance row's `id` as its own `id`, keeps `created_at`/`updated_at`, gets `meta = '{}'` (what `restore_to_board` wrote) and `order` by `updated_at DESC` (the order the Maintenance page showed).\n   - Re-point `projectinfo`: `SET repo_card_id = maintenance_id, maintenance_id = NULL` for the moved rows, in one statement (the exclusive CHECK holds throughout).\n   - A maintenance row whose repository already sits on a board is not moved (the unique index forbids a second card). Its `projectinfo` moves to the existing card when that card has none; otherwise it is deleted with the row. The number of such rows is reported as a `WARNING`. Production has 0.\n   - No board is created for a user whose rows were all skipped.\n2. Drop `move_to_maintenance(uuid, uuid, text, text)` and `restore_to_board(uuid, uuid, uuid, text, text, int)`.\n3. `projectinfo`: drop the three policies, delete rows still linked to maintenance, drop `projectinfo_target_exclusive_check`, drop column `maintenance_id` (takes `projectinfo_maintenance_id_key`, the FK and `idx_projectinfo_maintenance_id` with it), set `repo_card_id NOT NULL`, recreate the policies with the repocard → board path only (same names, same `(select auth.uid())` form as `20260208_reenable_rls_optimized_policies.sql`).\n4. `DROP TABLE maintenance` (its policies, trigger and index go with it).\n5. Update both generated type files (`src/lib/supabase/types.ts`, `src/lib/supabase/database.types.ts`).\n\nRollback: restore from Supabase PITR. A data-only dump of `maintenance` and `projectinfo` is taken to a local file before the merge.\n\nRehearsal before the merge: load a data dump of production into local Supabase at the pre-migration schema, apply the migration, and check: 1 new board `Maintenance`, 23 cards on it, 2 `projectinfo` rows re-pointed, 0 rows lost from `repocard`, table and column gone. A second scenario built by hand covers the overlap path (repository on a board and in Maintenance, with and without `projectinfo` on each side) and the re-run.\n\n","newText":"### 1. Migration `20261012090000_remove_maintenance_mode.sql`\n\nThe timestamp sorts after `20261011030000`, the newest migration. One `DO` block (atomic however the CLI wraps the file), `lock_timeout` 5s. Messages carry counts only.\n\nA second run after success does nothing: the block starts with `IF to_regclass('public.maintenance') IS NULL THEN RETURN`. A failed run changes nothing (one transaction), so there is no partial state to resume from.\n\nSteps, in this order:\n\n1. Lock `maintenance`, `projectinfo` and `repocard` in `ACCESS EXCLUSIVE` mode.\n2. Guard: count the entries whose repository is already on one of the user's boards (compared with `lower()`, the key of `repocard_unique_repo_per_user`) and the groups of entries of one user that differ only in letter case. When either count is above 0, raise an exception that carries the two counts and change nothing. No entry and no note is ever dropped by this migration.\n3. For each user with entries, in this order (the card triggers `set_repocard_user_id` and `check_repocard_status_board` read the board and the column):\n   - Board: name `Maintenance`, `position = COALESCE(MAX(position), -1) + 1` over the user's boards. A user who already has a board with that name still gets a new one; board names are not unique.\n   - Column: name `Archived`, default color, `order` 0, grid 0/0.\n   - Cards: one per entry. The card reuses the entry's `id`, keeps `created_at`/`updated_at`, gets `meta = '{}'` (what `restore_to_board` wrote) and `order` 0..n by `updated_at DESC NULLS LAST, id` (the order the Maintenance page showed).\n4. Re-point `projectinfo`: `SET repo_card_id = maintenance_id, maintenance_id = NULL` for every row with a `maintenance_id`, in one statement (the exclusive CHECK holds throughout). The trigger `update_projectinfo_updated_at` is disabled immediately before this statement and enabled again immediately after it, so `updated_at` is not rewritten.\n5. Drop `move_to_maintenance(uuid, uuid, text, text)` and `restore_to_board(uuid, uuid, uuid, text, text, integer)` with `IF EXISTS`.\n6. `projectinfo`: drop every policy on the table by looping over `pg_policies` (the migrations define two, \"Users can view their project info\" and \"Users can manage their project info\"; a count other than 2 is reported as a `WARNING`), drop `projectinfo_target_exclusive_check`, drop column `maintenance_id` (takes `projectinfo_maintenance_id_key`, the FK and `idx_projectinfo_maintenance_id` with it), set `repo_card_id NOT NULL`, recreate the two policies with the repocard → board path only (same names, same `(select auth.uid())` form as `20260208_reenable_rls_optimized_policies.sql`; the `FOR ALL` policy keeps a `WITH CHECK` equal to its `USING`).\n7. `DROP TABLE maintenance` (its policies, trigger and index go with it). The table is not kept under another name: the dump of section 6, step 5 is the copy.\n8. `RAISE NOTICE` with three counts: entries moved, boards created, `projectinfo` rows re-pointed. The table is gone after this, so the message in the job log is the record of what was moved.\n\nBecause the card reuses the entry's `id`, a note draft the browser saved under that id (the persisted `draft` slice) continues on the card.\n\nType files: edit `src/lib/supabase/types.ts` by hand (it carries hand-written aliases; remove the `maintenance` table, `projectinfo.maintenance_id`, the two functions and the `Maintenance` alias; `repo_card_id` becomes `string`). Delete `src/lib/supabase/database.types.ts`: `src/app/maintenance/page.tsx` is its only importer. Remove its entries from `knip.config.ts` and `eslint.config.mjs`.\n\nA user whose only data was Maintenance entries ends up with \"Maintenance\" as their only board, so the first-board creation on sign-in does not run for them. Accepted.\n\nRehearsal before the merge, on local Supabase. Every `--linked` command is run only after `supabase/.temp/project-ref` is confirmed to read `zfcwvzazzzsefbkztsyp` (production). The host has no `psql`; SQL goes through `docker exec -i supabase_db_gitbox psql -U postgres`.\n\nScenario A, rows built by hand:\n\n1. `supabase db reset --local --version 20261011030000 --no-seed` (the schema production has today).\n2. Insert: a user with two entries and no board; a user with one board, one card and one entry that has `projectinfo`.\n3. Guard, overlap: add an entry for the repository of the second user's card. `supabase migration up --local` fails with counts 1 and 0; no board was created, the table is still there. Delete that entry.\n4. Guard, letter case: add an entry that differs from an existing one only in letter case. `supabase migration up --local` fails with counts 0 and 1. Delete that entry.\n5. `supabase migration up --local` succeeds. Expect: the first user gets `Maintenance` at position 0 with 2 cards; the second user gets `Maintenance` after their board with 1 card that carries the entry's `projectinfo` with an unchanged `updated_at`; the `NOTICE` reads 3 entries, 2 boards, 1 `projectinfo` row.\n\nScenario B, a copy of production:\n\n6. `supabase db dump --linked --data-only --schema public --file <path>` with the path outside the repository and outside any synced directory. No `auth` data is dumped.\n7. Reset as in step 1, insert one stub `auth.users` row per user id the dump references, load the dump.\n8. `supabase migration up --local`. Check with count queries: 1 new board `Maintenance`, its card count equals the dumped `maintenance` row count, every `projectinfo` row that was on an entry is on the card with the same id and keeps its `updated_at`, `repocard` lost no row, table `maintenance` and column `maintenance_id` are gone, the two policies exist, `update_projectinfo_updated_at` is enabled (`tgenabled = 'O'`). The `NOTICE` reports the same numbers.\n9. Early return: `supabase migration repair --status reverted 20261012090000 --local`, then `supabase migration up --local` again. The block returns at its first statement and the counts of step 8 are unchanged. (Without the repair the CLI skips the file and the early return is never executed.)\n\nThe verification query (rehearsal step 8 and section 6, step 7):\n\n```sql\nSELECT\n  to_regclass('public.maintenance') IS NULL AS table_gone,\n  NOT EXISTS (SELECT 1 FROM information_schema.columns\n    WHERE table_schema = 'public' AND table_name = 'projectinfo'\n      AND column_name = 'maintenance_id') AS column_gone,\n  (SELECT count(*) FROM board WHERE name = 'Maintenance') AS boards_named_maintenance,\n  (SELECT count(*) FROM repocard r JOIN board b ON b.id = r.board_id\n    WHERE b.name = 'Maintenance') AS cards_on_them,\n  (SELECT count(*) FROM projectinfo p JOIN repocard r ON r.id = p.repo_card_id\n    JOIN board b ON b.id = r.board_id WHERE b.name = 'Maintenance') AS projectinfo_on_them,\n  (SELECT count(*) FROM pg_policies\n    WHERE schemaname = 'public' AND tablename = 'projectinfo') AS projectinfo_policies,\n  (SELECT tgenabled FROM pg_trigger WHERE tgname = 'update_projectinfo_updated_at'\n    AND tgrelid = 'public.projectinfo'::regclass) AS projectinfo_trigger;\n```\n\nThe database of scenario B stays in place for the local check and the screenshots (section 6, step 3). `pnpm db:reset` puts the local database back to the seed afterwards.\n\n"},{"oldText":"### 2. Server\n\n- `repo-cards.ts`: delete `moveToMaintenance`, `restoreToBoard` and helpers only they use.\n- Delete `maintenance-project-info.ts`.\n- `shared-project-info.ts` → `project-info-core.ts`: remove `FkConfig`; core functions take the card id and use `repo_card_id` directly. `project-info.ts` stays the `'use server'` boundary. Update every import.\n- `board-data.ts`: delete `getUserMaintenanceRepoIdentifiers` and `maintenanceRepoIdentifiers`; `board/[id]/page.tsx` awaits the board bundle only.\n- `repo-card-duplicates.ts`: placements are board placements only. `RepoPlacements` becomes `BoardPlacement[]` (a wrapper object with one key says nothing); `SkippedReason` loses `'maintenance'`; the placement lookup is one query.\n- `account/page.tsx`: drop the maintenance count query.\n- Rate-limit keys, Sentry contexts and security-event names that only these actions used are removed with them.\n\n","newText":"### 2. Server\n\n- `repo-cards.ts`: delete `moveToMaintenance` and `restoreToBoard`. `getUserBoardsWithStatusLists` stays: Move to Another Board uses it.\n- Delete `maintenance-project-info.ts`.\n- `shared-project-info.ts` → `project-info-core.ts`: remove `FkConfig`; core functions take the card id and use `repo_card_id` directly. `project-info.ts` stays the `'use server'` boundary. Update every import.\n- `board-data.ts`: delete `getUserMaintenanceRepoIdentifiers` and `maintenanceRepoIdentifiers`; `board/[id]/page.tsx` awaits the board bundle only.\n- `repo-card-duplicates.ts`: placements are board placements only. `RepoPlacements` becomes `BoardPlacement[]` (a wrapper object with one key says nothing); `SkippedReason` loses `'maintenance'`; the placement lookup is one query.\n- `account/page.tsx`: drop the maintenance count query.\n\n"},{"oldText":"### 3. Client\n\n- Delete `src/app/maintenance/**`, `RestoreToBoardDialog` (+ stories), `DeleteMaintenanceDialog`, `ROUTES.MAINTENANCE`.\n- `OverflowMenu`: remove \"Move to Maintenance\", `context`, `onRestoreToBoard`, `onDelete`, the destructive maintenance item and the generic `TId`; ids are `RepoCardId`.\n- Remove the `onMoveToMaintenance`/`onMaintenance` prop chain and `handleMoveToMaintenance`.\n- `NoteModal`: `RepoCardId` instead of generic `TId`. `brands.ts`: delete `MaintenanceId`, `toMaintenanceId`, `CardIdentifier` (callers use `RepoCardId`).\n- `HeldRepositoryList`: a held repository is always on another board; the `location` union collapses to the board fields.\n- `AddRepositoryCombobox`: remove `maintenanceRepoIdentifiers` and the page-load fallback set. When placements are unknown, only repositories on the current board are hidden (the server still refuses duplicates by the unique index).\n- Sidebar: remove the \"Maintenance Mode\" item. Command Palette: remove \"Go to Maintenance\" and the `G M` hint. Shortcuts help: same.\n- `/account`: two tiles (Boards, Repository Cards) in a two-column grid.\n- Landing `FeaturesSection`: replace the \"Maintenance Mode\" card with \"Multiple Boards\" (icon `LayoutGrid` or the closest existing one): one board per context, move a card between boards with its notes and links.\n- `/maintenance` returns the app's 404 page. No redirect: nothing links to it and the product has 2 users.\n\n","newText":"### 3. Client\n\n- Move the `BoardOption` type from `RestoreToBoardDialog.tsx` into `MoveToAnotherBoardDialog.tsx`, its remaining user. The move dialog's empty state (\"No other boards available. Create another board first.\") gains a link to `/boards/new`: with Maintenance gone, moving a card to another board is the only way to set a repository aside with its notes, and a user with one board has to be able to get there from the dialog. Then delete `src/app/maintenance/**`, `RestoreToBoardDialog` (+ stories), `DeleteMaintenanceDialog`, `ROUTES.MAINTENANCE`.\n- `OverflowMenu`: remove \"Move to Maintenance\", `context`, `onRestoreToBoard`, `onDelete`, the destructive maintenance item and the generic `TId`; ids are `RepoCardId`.\n- Remove the `onMoveToMaintenance`/`onMaintenance` prop chain and `handleMoveToMaintenance`. `useOptimisticCardAction` keeps its remaining caller, Remove from Board; its docstring says so. This default stands unless the engineering review records a different decision.\n- `brands.ts`: delete `MaintenanceId`, `toMaintenanceId`, `CardIdentifier`. `NoteModal`, `useNoteModalDraft` and `draftSlice` use `RepoCardId` instead of a generic id.\n- `HeldRepositoryList`: a held repository is always on another board; the `location` union collapses to the board fields.\n- `AddRepositoryCombobox`: remove `maintenanceRepoIdentifiers` and the page-load fallback set. When placements are unknown, only repositories on the current board are hidden (the server still refuses duplicates by the unique index).\n- Sidebar: remove the \"Maintenance Mode\" item. Command Palette: remove \"Go to Maintenance\" and the `G M - Go to Maintenance` part of the shortcuts hint.\n- `/account`: two tiles (Boards, Repository Cards) in a two-column grid.\n- Landing `FeaturesSection`: the fourth card keeps the promise the old one made (set finished work aside without losing its history) and says how it works now. Title \"Move Between Boards\", icon `LayoutGrid`, text: park finished or paused projects on a board of their own; notes, links and comments move with the card. The section keeps four cards because its grid is two columns wide; three would leave a hole. The design review settles the final wording.\n- `/maintenance` returns the app's 404 page. No redirect: nothing links to it and the product has 2 users.\n\n"},{"oldText":"### 4. Tests and fixtures\n\n- Delete the 4 `maintenance-*.spec.ts` files and `MaintenanceClient.test.tsx`.\n- Remove maintenance cases from the other e2e specs and unit tests; keep every case that tests board behavior. `one-repo-across-boards.spec.ts` and the placement unit tests keep the cross-board cases.\n- `seed.sql`: drop maintenance rows, their `projectinfo`, and the `maintenance_id` column from inserts. `db-query.ts`: drop maintenance helpers. `mocks/handlers`: drop maintenance handlers and data. Stories: drop maintenance variants.\n- New tests:\n  - Unit: `OverflowMenu` no longer offers \"Move to Maintenance\"; Sidebar and Command Palette have no Maintenance entry; `/account` shows two tiles; placement split without maintenance.\n  - E2E: `/maintenance` shows the 404 page for a signed-in user; the card menu lists Move to Another Board and Remove from Board and no Maintenance item.\n  - `src/tests/unit/no-maintenance-mode.test.ts`: reads the source tree and fails when `maintenance` (case-insensitive) appears under `src`, `e2e`, `mocks` or `supabase/seed.sql`, outside an allowlist (this test, `.github/release.yml` is out of the scanned paths). Keeps the removal from growing back.\n  - Migration: a script under `supabase/tests/` (plain SQL run through `psql` in the local database container) builds the scenarios in section 1 and asserts the result. Run by hand and in the rehearsal; not wired into CI.\n\n","newText":"### 4. Tests and fixtures\n\n- Delete the 4 `maintenance-*.spec.ts` files and `MaintenanceClient.test.tsx`.\n- Remove maintenance cases from the other e2e specs and unit tests; keep every case that tests board behavior. `one-repo-across-boards.spec.ts` and the placement unit tests keep the cross-board cases. A case that used a Maintenance entry only as \"some repository held elsewhere\" uses a card on another board instead.\n- `command-palette.spec.ts` uses \"Go to Maintenance\" as the unrelated command that a search for \"boards\" must filter out. It switches to \"Go to Settings\", so the assertion can still fail.\n- `ssr-hydration.spec.ts` lists the landing feature headings; its 'Maintenance Mode' entry becomes the new card's title, so the new card is asserted. `server-rendered-client-components.test.ts` drops its `MaintenanceClient` entry.\n- `seed.sql`: drop maintenance rows, their `projectinfo`, the `maintenance_id` column from inserts and the header lines that list them. `db-query.ts`: drop maintenance helpers. `mocks/handlers`: drop maintenance handlers and data. Stories: drop maintenance variants.\n- New tests, each for behavior that exists after the change. No test asserts that a removed thing is absent: nothing could bring it back by accident, and such a test would itself contain the word the grep forbids.\n  - Unit: the move dialog with no other board shows the link to `/boards/new`; `/account` shows the Boards and Repository Cards tiles; the placement split and the 23505 mapping with board placements only.\n  - E2E: a card moved to another board and back keeps its note (the park and bring back path); the picker lists a repository of another board under \"Already placed elsewhere\".\n  - Migration: no committed test. The rehearsal in section 1 is the check; its counts are recorded in the PR.\n\n"},{"oldText":"### 5. Docs\n\n- `SPEC.md`: remove the Maintenance sections, routes, data model and wireframes; describe archiving as \"a board of your own\".\n- `README.md`, `CLAUDE.md` (schema is 6 tables, verification rules without Maintenance, server action list, board data paragraph), `TODOS.md` (delete \"Enforce board vs Maintenance exclusivity in the database\"; reword the entries that mention Maintenance).\n- `CHANGELOG.md` and version through `/ship`.\n\n","newText":"### 5. Docs\n\n- `SPEC.md`: remove the Maintenance sections, routes, data model and wireframes; describe archiving as \"a board of your own\".\n- `README.md`, `CLAUDE.md` (schema is 6 tables, route tree, verification rules without Maintenance, the fixture-auth steps that open `/maintenance`, server action list, board data paragraph), `TODOS.md` (delete \"Enforce board vs Maintenance exclusivity in the database\"; reword the entries that mention Maintenance, restore or the restore dialog; add \"Refresh a card's GitHub metadata\": `meta` is a snapshot from the moment a card is added and nothing updates it).\n- Retake `public/screenshots/app-screenshot.png` (1200×670, README) and `public/screenshots/social-preview.png` (1280×640). Both show the owner's \"Laststance\" board in the dark theme with \"Maintenance Mode\" in the sidebar. Same board and theme, taken from the local copy of production; only public repositories in the frame.\n- `CHANGELOG.md` and version through `/ship`.\n\n"},{"oldText":"### 6. Rollout\n\n1. `pnpm test`, `pnpm lint`, `pnpm build`, `pnpm typecheck`, `pnpm e2e:parallel`.\n2. Rehearse the migration on a local copy of production data.\n3. Production check as `ryota-murakami`: board \"Maintenance\" has 23 cards, the 2 notes open, sidebar/palette/account/landing changed, `/maintenance` is 404, picker lists a repository of the Maintenance board under \"Already placed elsewhere\", Move to Another Board works on a scratch board.\n\nKnown window: the migration finishes about 35 s after the merge and the Vercel build takes a few minutes. Until the new build is live, the old code's board page queries a table that no longer exists. Accepted (2 users, night rollout); the alternative is two PRs.\n\n","newText":"### 6. Rollout\n\n1. `pnpm test`, `pnpm lint`, `pnpm build`, `pnpm typecheck`, `pnpm e2e:parallel` (the E2E run resets the local database, so it comes first).\n2. Rehearse the migration (section 1, scenarios A and B).\n3. Local check as `ryota-murakami` on `http://localhost:3008`, on the database of scenario B. Sign in first (local GoTrue gives the user a new id), then re-point the copied rows from the stub owner id to the local id, in this order: `board.user_id`, then `repocard.user_id` (its trigger copies the owner from the board and fires only when the card row itself is updated), then `user_settings` and `user_link_presets` (delete the local user's own row first where the key would collide). Check: the migrated `Maintenance` board and its notes, Move to Another Board in both directions, Remove from Board, the picker's \"Already placed elsewhere\" for a repository on the `Maintenance` board, repositories added from the picker to a new board (two columns, one private or organization repository), sidebar, palette, `/account`, `/maintenance`. Then the two screenshots of section 5; before they are committed, each image is looked at once more for a private repository name.\n4. `/review`, `/design-review`, `/ship` (draft PR first; CI runs on PRs only). One PR, three commits that each pass `pnpm typecheck`: (a) server and client removal with their tests, the move dialog link and the landing card; (b) the migration, `types.ts`, the seed and the fixtures; (c) docs and screenshots. The migration is reviewed as its own commit.\n5. Right before the merge: run the audit query on production again and note the `entries` count. Expected `entries_also_on_a_board` and `case_variant_groups`: 0. Nothing writes to Maintenance between this step and the migration run: the owner is the only user with entries and is away, and no check uses Move to Maintenance or Restore on production. Take a data-only dump of production's `public` schema to a local file outside the repository. Confirm that the owner has at least two repositories on no board (the scratch boards of step 9 need them). No check on production or on a preview deployment adds a repository that is in Maintenance to a board before the merge (the new picker does not know about Maintenance, and previews use the production database). If the audit shows an overlap anyway, the migration would refuse to run; remove the board card the check added, then merge.\n6. Merge. The merge runs `supabase db push` on production at once and Vercel deploys.\n7. Wait for the `Supabase Production Migration` run of the merge commit to finish green and read the migration's `NOTICE` in its log. Then run the verification query on production: table and column gone, one board named `Maintenance`, its card count equal to \"entries moved\" in the `NOTICE` and to the count noted in step 5, the `projectinfo` rows on those cards, 2 policies, trigger `O`.\n8. Wait for the Vercel production deployment of the merge commit.\n9. Production check as `ryota-murakami`: board \"Maintenance\" shows its cards, a known repository is found on it with the browser's find-in-page and its note opens, sidebar/palette/account/landing changed, `/maintenance` is 404, the picker lists a repository of the Maintenance board under \"Already placed elsewhere\". On two scratch boards: add repositories, Move to Another Board between them, Remove from Board; delete both boards afterwards. The scratch boards need repositories that are on no board; after the migration every former Maintenance repository is placed. If the picker offers none, the add, move and remove flows are reported as not verified on production, with that reason.\n10. Fill `meta` of the migrated cards. Maintenance never stored it, so those cards show a bare title next to cards that carry a description, stars and language. For each migrated card read the repository with `gh api` as `ryota-murakami` and collect the fields `addRepositoriesToBoard` stores. Write them in one `DO` block through `supabase db query --linked`: the values travel as one JSON document in a dollar-quoted literal (no quote escaping), and `update_repocard_updated_at` is disabled before the UPDATE and enabled after it, so the cards keep their `updated_at`. Tried on the local copy first. By hand, nothing committed. A repository GitHub no longer returns keeps its empty `meta`. Check afterwards: the number of cards on the board with a non-empty `meta` equals the number of repositories GitHub returned, the trigger is enabled, and the board in the browser shows descriptions.\n\nWhen the migration run fails on `lock_timeout`: run it again with `gh workflow run supabase-production.yml` (the workflow has `workflow_dispatch`). When it fails for another reason: the database is unchanged (one transaction) and the failed migration is not recorded, so the fix is an edit of the same migration file in a new commit on `main`, which triggers the run again. Meanwhile the new build runs on the old schema and works; the Maintenance entries are intact and not reachable from the UI until the migration succeeds.\n\nWhen the migration succeeds and the result is wrong: rebuild the affected cards by hand from the rows in the dump of step 5 (its `maintenance` inserts target a table that no longer exists, so the file is a source of values, not something to replay). Point-in-time recovery is the last resort; it also rolls back every other write made since.\n\nKnown window: in earlier runs the migration finished about 35 s after the merge (estimate), and the Vercel build takes a few minutes. Until the new build is live, the old code runs on the new schema. The board page keeps working (`getUserMaintenanceRepoIdentifiers` ignores the query error and returns an empty list), and so do notes, links and comments of board cards (the old code reads and writes them by `repo_card_id` only, `shared-project-info.ts:236` and `:299`). The old Maintenance page shows an empty list, Move to Maintenance fails with an error toast, and the picker's placement lookup shows its error state. A tab opened before the deploy keeps the old client; its server actions belong to the old build, and a reload brings the new one. Accepted (2 users, night rollout); the alternative is two PRs.\n\n"},{"oldText":"## Out of scope\n\n- A board preset or a special board type for archiving. A board is a board.\n- Renaming or reordering the migrated board for the owner; they can rename it.\n- Editing old migrations or `docs/plans/2026-10-11-*.md` (history).\n- `.github/release.yml` \"🔧 Maintenance\" release-notes category (unrelated meaning).\n","newText":"## Out of scope\n\n- A board preset or a special board type for archiving. A board is a board.\n- Renaming or reordering the migrated board for the owner; they can rename it.\n- Resolving an entry whose repository is also on a board, or entries that differ only in letter case. The migration refuses to run instead; production has neither.\n- An archived flag on cards or boards. It would keep a second state that every board query, the picker and the counts have to filter; the issue asks for a plain board.\n- A \"refresh from GitHub\" action for card metadata. It is a feature of its own (about ten files with its tests); it goes to TODOS.md. The cards this migration creates get their `meta` once, by hand (section 6, step 10).\n- Editing old migrations or `docs/plans/2026-10-11-*.md` (history).\n- `.github/release.yml` \"🔧 Maintenance\" release-notes category (unrelated meaning).\n\n## Requirements added in review\n"}]} -->

<!-- autoplan-accepted:ceo -->

- No source-scanning test bans the word "maintenance". Instead the `git grep` of the Success list runs once before the PR is marked ready, and its output (expected: no lines) goes into the PR body.
- The migration scenarios of section 1 are built by hand in local Supabase during the rehearsal. No scenario script is committed. Before and after counts go into the PR body.
- The browser check on production does not start before the migration run is green, the count-only query matches, and the Vercel deployment of the merge commit is live (section 6, steps 7 to 9).
- No check before the merge, on production or on a preview deployment, adds a repository that is in Maintenance to a board.
- Rehearsal scenario A also reads `projectinfo` as each of the two users (role `authenticated` with that user's JWT claims) after the migration: each sees only the rows of their own cards.
- The dumps of production are written to `~/gitbox-backups/` with mode 600. That directory is outside the repository and outside `~/.gstack`, which is synced.
- The `meta` fill sends its values inside a dollar-quoted literal whose tag is random per run, and it refuses to send when the JSON contains that tag.

<!-- /autoplan-accepted:ceo -->

#### 0I. Temporal interrogation

```
HOUR 1 (foundations):   The migration must drop the three projectinfo policies before the
                        column, or DROP COLUMN fails on the dependency. seed.sql runs after
                        migrations, so it has to match the new schema in the same commit.
HOUR 2-3 (core logic):  Removing the generic TId from OverflowMenu and NoteModal ripples into
                        every caller's props. RepoPlacements → BoardPlacement[] touches the
                        hook, the action, the picker and their tests.
HOUR 4-5 (integration): e2e specs that seed a maintenance row to get a "held" picker row need a
                        second board instead. MSW handlers and stories reference the table.
                        Both type files are hand-maintained and must agree.
HOUR 6+ (polish/tests): SPEC.md wireframes, CLAUDE.md verification rules, TODOS.md rewording,
                        unused imports (Archive icon, ROUTES entry) that lint will flag.
```

Effort: human team about 2 days, CC + gstack about 2 hours of work plus CI and rollout waits. No feasibility blocker.

#### 0.5 Dual voices

Input bound for both: `autoplan-ceo-BzGBCU/ceo-implementation.md`, sha `293b09c3…28e3b4`.

**Claude CEO subagent** (native, completed, `INPUT: ceo 293b09c3…` matches). No critical flaw; 10 findings:

1. Medium. "Archive = a board" was never weighed against an archived flag; the owner's board list and card count change (27 → 50); a column "Archived" inside a board "Maintenance" repeats the label.
2. High. Migration step 5 deleted `projectinfo` of entries that were not moved, behind a `WARNING`. Make it fail closed.
3. High. The `meta` fill is a hand-run production write and hides a product gap: nothing ever refreshes `meta`. Build a refresh action, or at least make the fill transactional.
4. Medium. One PR mixes a mechanical deletion with a data move that cannot be undone. Split into commits; review the migration on its own.
5. Medium. "Old build on new schema" was argued for one function only; the note path and persisted drafts were unexamined.
6. Medium. `DROP TABLE` has no soft landing (rename to a legacy table). Either accept the dump as the copy and say so, or schedule the drop.
7. Medium. Production verification may be reported "not verified" for the flows this PR changes most; screenshots come from real owner data.
8. Medium. The re-pointing of copied rows to the local user is a brittle ceremony.
9. Medium. The migration handles cases production does not have; the fail-closed fix removes most of it.
10. Medium. New landing copy is invented inside a refactor; a deleted feature does not need a replacement card.

**Codex CEO voice** (outside, `OUTSIDE_STATUS: completed provider=codex host=claude`, verdict: findings, P1). 5 findings, recommendation "Revise before implementation because the plan verifies schema removal and data transfer but does not establish that ordinary boards preserve archive retrieval and first-time archiving":

1. High. The central premise "a board does the same job" is unverified: the Maintenance page has search, sort and a list view (`useMaintenanceViewState.ts:29`), and it holds 23 of 50 repositories. Add the acceptance "find a half-remembered repository, read its note, bring it back", or state the reduction.
2. High. Only the migrated owner gets a destination. A user with one board meets "No other boards available. Create another board first." (`MoveToAnotherBoardDialog.tsx:272`), and Remove from Board deletes notes.
3. Medium. Success is measured by a deleted string, and the planned absence tests would themselves contain the word the grep forbids.
4. Medium. The plan moves maintenance work to owner-only manual operations (hand-built scenarios, id re-pointing, uncommitted `meta` fill, manual restore).
5. Medium. The landing change fills a layout hole with a feature name that overlaps "Kanban Board"; pitch the outcome or drop to three cards.
