### Phase 2.5: DX review (mode: DX POLISH, /autoplan override)

Methodology read: `autoplan-dx-methodology-KeJMhc/methodology.md`, ranges 1-600, 601-1200, 1201-1800, 1801-2130 of 2130 lines (through EOF). Input bound for both voices: `autoplan-dx-eW8qAZ/dx-implementation.md`, sha `5a9e944b…5b0047`. Hall of Fame sections read: Pass 1 to Pass 8.

#### Step 0: DX investigation

Product type (auto-detected): a web application, not a developer tool. Its developer-facing surface is the repository: the setup docs, the scripts, the migration procedure, and the contracts of the server actions. The DX scope check matched 14 terms (threshold 2), so the phase runs.

```
TARGET DEVELOPER PERSONA
========================
Who:       the owner-operator and the coding agents that work for them; second, a contributor who clones from GitHub
Context:   changes a server action or writes a migration, then rolls it out; a merge to main migrates production at once
Tolerance: an agent follows the docs to the letter, so a wrong command in a doc gets executed; a human gives up after about 15 minutes of setup
Expects:   scripts that do what the docs say, a migration procedure that names real commands, errors that say what to do next
```

Evidence: `README.md` "Getting Started" (local setup for one developer), `CLAUDE.md` (rules written for agents), `package.json` scripts, one owner and 2 users.

Developer perspective (0B), first person:

> I am an agent the owner asked to remove a feature and roll the change out while they sleep. I open CLAUDE.md first, because it tells me how this repository wants to be worked on. The migration procedure says: test on dev with `supabase link --project-ref jqtxjzdxczqwsrvevmyk`. I run `supabase projects list` and that project is not there; only production is. So the one documented rehearsal step is gone, and a merge to main runs the migration on production at once. I write my own rehearsal. I dump production's data, load it locally and run the migration. It passes. Then I notice the copy carries only data: the policies and triggers I tested against came from the repository, not from production. Did anyone ever change a policy in the dashboard? I cannot tell from here. The migration reports three counts. Twenty-three entries in, twenty-three cards out. But are they the same twenty-three, with their notes? A count does not say. And when the guard refuses on production at night, the log gives me two numbers and no next step. In the app I create a board from the move dialog; the column insert fails; I press Create again and now there are two boards with one name.

Observed: the dead project ref, `version: latest` in the workflow, `createBoard` in two requests. Predicted: a policy changed in the dashboard, the failed column insert.

Competitive benchmark (0C). Clock: from a fresh clone to a signed-in board that holds a repository. No time was measured; the boundaries differ (GitBox needs a GitHub OAuth app).

| Tool                                | Start → result                              | Time + evidence type                                                                                                                                                       | DX choice                                                             | Source                              |
| ----------------------------------- | ------------------------------------------- | -------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | --------------------------------------------------------------------- | ----------------------------------- |
| GitBox today                        | clone → signed-in board with a repository   | estimated 15 to 20 min the first time, about 10 actions (counted from the README and the scripts); about 2 min for a repeat start (`pnpm db:start`, `pnpm dev`), estimated | scripts wrap the CLI; the docs contradict the scripts in three places | `README.md`, `package.json`         |
| vercel/nextjs-subscription-payments | clone → local app on local Supabase         | not reported; 6 to 8 documented actions                                                                                                                                    | every Supabase step is a package script                               | its README (web search, 2026-10-12) |
| Supabase CLI local stack            | `supabase start` → API URL and keys printed | not reported; bounded by Docker image downloads                                                                                                                            | one command prints the credentials                                    | reference knowledge, not searched   |
| GitBox after this plan              | same                                        | same estimate; the ordered README removes one predicted failure (database start before the file it reads)                                                                  | the docs match the scripts                                            | section 5 of the plan               |

Research: Aside is not installed; one web search ran (sanitized query, no repository data).

TTHW target (auto-decided): Competitive, 2 to 5 minutes, for a repeat start on a prepared machine. The estimate meets it. The first-time path stays an estimate (T11).

Magical moment (0D). For this plan the developer is an operator. The moment: the migration finishes and a `diff` of two manifest files prints nothing; or it refuses with a sentence that says what to do. Delivery vehicle: the runbook and the manifest, built from tools already in use (`supabase db query`, `diff`). No hosted service, no new API.

Mode (0E): DX POLISH by the `/autoplan` override.

Developer journey (0F):

```
STAGE           | DEVELOPER DOES                           | FRICTION POINTS                                              | STATUS
----------------|------------------------------------------|--------------------------------------------------------------|--------
1. Discover     | reads README features and CLAUDE.md      | README lists the removed feature                             | fixed
2. Install      | pnpm install, env files, pnpm db:start   | db start comes before the env file it reads; pnpm 10.26.2    | fixed
                |                                          | in the README, 11.1.2 pinned; bare supabase commands         |
3. Hello World  | pnpm dev, sign in, add a repository      | first run bounded by Docker downloads and OAuth registration | ok, not measured
4. Real Usage   | calls createBoard, adds a preset         | board without columns after a failed insert; PRESET_IDS      | fixed
                |                                          | serves the schema and the selector at once                   |
5. Debug        | reads a refused migration, a failed action| two counts and no next step; "An unexpected error occurred"  | fixed
6. Upgrade      | merges, production migrates              | procedure names a deleted project; data-only rehearsal;      | fixed
                |                                          | verification by counts; no commands on record                |
```

First-time developer report (0G):

```
FIRST-TIME DEVELOPER REPORT
============================
Persona: contributor from GitHub
Attempting: GitBox getting started

CONFUSION LOG:
T+0:00  Opens README "Getting Started". It says pnpm 10.26.2; package.json pins 11.1.2. (observed)
T+1:00  pnpm install, cp .env.local.example .env. The example's first step says "supabase start". (observed)
T+3:00  Runs pnpm db:start as the README says. The script sources supabase/.env, which the README
        explains two sections later. Predicted: the script stops, the file is missing.
T+6:00  Registers a GitHub OAuth app, writes supabase/.env, restarts with the README's bare
        "supabase stop && supabase start". CLAUDE.md says a bare start does not load the credentials.
        (documented; not run here)
T+12:00 Docker images are down (estimate). supabase status, copies URL and keys into .env.
T+15:00 pnpm dev, signs in, the first board appears, adds a repository. Succeeded.
```

Addressed: the README order, the version and the script commands (decision 59). Not addressed: the time itself (T11).

#### Step 0.5: dual voices

Running the outside voice automatically (standard step). Disable: `gstack-config set codex_reviews disabled`.

CODEX SAYS (DX — developer experience challenge), 6 findings:

1. High. The inline create flow assumes a stronger `createBoard` contract than the code gives: board and columns are two operations, a failed second one leaves a board, the wrapper turns the failure into "An unexpected error occurred", and a retry makes another board. A reload after a successful create is a second partial result.
2. High. The rollout names its hard operations in prose (fixture rows, stub users, dump load, ownership remapping, the RLS read, the metadata update, rebuilding cards). A second operator has to invent the SQL. The workflow installs CLI `latest`, so a local rehearsal does not show what CI runs. Wants one committed runbook with executable blocks and a pinned CLI version.
3. Medium. The first-run sequence in the README is broken and "under five minutes" is unproven: pnpm 10.26.2 against 11.1.2, `pnpm db:start` before `supabase/.env` is explained, bare `supabase stop && supabase start`, `.env.local.example` starting with bare `supabase start`. About ten actions from zero.
4. Medium. The verification query shows removal, counts, policy count and trigger state. It does not show that each entry became the card with the same id, that the old cards are all there, that every `projectinfo` row kept content and timestamp, or that the counted board is the one this migration made.
5. Medium. Guard errors name the kind of failure and give no way to find the rows. "Edit the migration" is the wrong advice for a refusal.
6. Medium. Unknown policies get a warning and are then dropped. That is schema drift turned into an unreviewed permission change.

Recommendation line: revise before implementation, because inline board creation can persist partial results and the rollout still needs operators to invent critical SQL without executable proof of data preservation.

CLAUDE SUBAGENT (DX — independent review), 16 findings (2 High, 9 Medium, 5 Low):

- H1: `createBoard` is not atomic and the dialog reloads without need (same as Codex 1). H2: rehearsal B is data only and cannot catch schema drift; the policy WARNING should be an exception.
- M1: failure messages are counts only, no cause, fix or pointer. M2: the `blank` preset is misnamed and `PRESET_IDS` feeds both `z.enum` and the selector. M3: no way to create a board in the dialog when other boards exist. M4: the local re-pointing has no SQL. M5: the rehearsal cannot be replayed; CLI versions differ; bare `supabase` commands. M6: no copy-paste runbook; CLAUDE.md names a development project that may be gone. M7: no practical undo. M8: one commit mixes deletion and new behavior. M9: the grep leaves the migration pattern nowhere to be written down.
- L1: an old local database can hit the guard. L2: screenshot viewport and scale. L3: check the `gh` account before the metadata fill. L4: say in the TODO that the PWA has no find-in-page. L5: `/maintenance` 404 is not a dead end (no change).

Scores given by the subagent: getting started 7, ergonomics 6, errors 5, documentation 6, escape hatches 5.

```
DX DUAL VOICES — CONSENSUS TABLE:
  Dimension                           Claude  Codex  Consensus
  1. Getting started < 5 min?          yes     no     DISAGREE (T11)
  2. API/CLI naming guessable?         no      no     CONFIRMED (createBoard contract)
  3. Error messages actionable?        no      no     CONFIRMED (refusal text, generic action error)
  4. Docs findable & complete?         no      no     CONFIRMED (no commands on record)
  5. Upgrade path safe?                no      no     CONFIRMED (policy drop on drift, weak proof)
  6. Dev environment friction-free?    no      no     CONFIRMED (CLI version, bare commands)
CONFIRMED = native + outside agree; primary cannot replace outside. DISAGREE → taste.
```

5 of 6 confirmed, 1 disagreement. Single-voice High finding: native H2 (schema comparison). It was checked against the plan (the copy in scenario B is `--data-only`) and accepted.

Claims checked in the code before they were accepted: `createBoard` inserts the board and then calls `createStatusListsFromPreset` in a second request (`src/lib/actions/board.ts:448-463`); the guard returns "An unexpected error occurred" for any thrown error that is not an `ActionUserError` (`auth-guard.ts:66`); `PRESET_IDS` is read by `presetIdSchema` (`validations/board.ts:50`) and by `PresetSelector.tsx:41`; `supabase projects list` returns production only; `package.json` pins `pnpm@11.1.2`; `pnpm db:start` is `cd supabase && . .env && supabase start`; E2E runs against the real local database with a valid JWT, so a database function is exercised there without a mock.

#### Pass 1: Getting started. 5/10 → 7/10

- 1A (Codex 3). The README setup is out of order and names a pnpm version the repository does not use. Fixed in section 5: one ordered path, the pinned version, the script commands.
- 1B (Codex 3). No measured time. T11: stays an estimate. A 10 here is a measured fresh clone under five minutes; this plan does not change the setup path, and the first run is bounded by Docker downloads and registering an OAuth app.

#### Pass 2: API design. 5/10 → 8/10

- 2A (both voices, High). `createBoard` can leave a board without columns and says so with a generic message. Decision 50: one database function creates both and returns them; decision 64: the failure text says what happened and what to do.
- 2B (native M2). `blank` names a preset that has a column, and one array decides both what the server accepts and what the page lists. Decision 51: `single-column`, `SELECTABLE_PRESET_IDS` next to `PRESET_IDS`.
- 2C (native M3). With other boards present the dialog cannot make a new one. T9: a "New board…" button opens the same form.
- 2D. The returned shape is the one the dialog already lists (`id`, `name`, `statusLists` with id, name, color), so no second read and no mapping.

#### Pass 3: Error messages. 4/10 → 8/10

Three paths traced:

| Path                                | Today (plan before this phase)                          | After                                                                                                                                                                  |
| ----------------------------------- | ------------------------------------------------------- | ---------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| The guard refuses on production     | a message with two counts                               | label `remove_maintenance_mode:`, the counts, `DETAIL` "Nothing was changed.", `HINT` with the fix and the command to run again; the runbook lists the rows in private |
| `createBoard` fails in the database | "An unexpected error occurred", and a board may be left | "The board was not created. Try again.", nothing left behind                                                                                                           |
| `projectinfo` has an unknown policy | a `WARNING`, then every policy is dropped               | a refusal with the count found and the hint to compare schemas                                                                                                         |

No error carries a link. The `HINT` names the commands, and the PR body links the runbook. That is why this is an 8 and not a 10.

#### Pass 4: Documentation. 5/10 → 8/10

- 4A (Codex 2, native M4 to M6). The hard steps were prose. T10: a committed runbook document with blocks that can be pasted; results in the PR body.
- 4B (native M6, checked). `CLAUDE.md` and `SPEC.md` send the reader to a development project that no longer exists. Decision 59.
- 4C (native M9). The pattern this migration uses had nowhere to live, because the grep keeps the feature's name out of the docs. Decision 63: a list in general words in `CLAUDE.md`, the history in `CHANGELOG.md`.
- 4D (Codex 3). README, see 1A.

#### Pass 5: Upgrade path. 6/10 → 9/10

- 5A (native H2). The rehearsal tested the repository's schema, not production's. Decision 55: compare the two schema dumps first.
- 5B (both voices). Decision 54: refuse on an unknown policy set, drop the two by name.
- 5C (Codex 4). Decision 56: a manifest compared before and after, with destination boards found through card ids.
- 5D (native M7). Decision 61: no reverse migration; the manifest and the dump repair forward.
- 5E (Codex 5). Three failure cases told apart in the rollout text: lock timeout, refusal, defect. A refusal needs no commit.

#### Pass 6: Developer environment. 6/10 → 8/10

- 6A (both voices). Decision 58: rehearse on the CLI version the workflow installs.
- 6B (native M5). Local resets go through the same wrapper as `pnpm db:reset`.
- 6C (native L1). A local database that still holds old rows can hit the guard on `pnpm db:start`; the `HINT` names `pnpm db:reset`.
- 6D (native M8). Decision 62: commits keep removal and new behavior apart.
- 6E. The new function is exercised by one E2E test against the real local database; unit tests mock the client.

#### Pass 7: Community. 5/10 → 5/10

Public repository, MIT license, features tracked as GitHub issues, no `CONTRIBUTING.md`. Neither voice raised anything and this plan does not touch it. No issues found; the score is the honest state, not a target of this change.

#### Pass 8: Measurement. 3/10 → 5/10

Nothing measures setup time and nothing is added (T11). What this plan does record: CLI versions, counts, the schema diff and the manifest diff, all in the PR body, so `/devex-review` can compare the plan with what happened.

#### DX scorecard

```
+====================================================================+
|              DX PLAN REVIEW — SCORECARD                             |
+====================================================================+
| Dimension            | Score  | Prior  | Trend  |
```

"Prior" is the plan as it entered this phase; no earlier DX review exists for this project. Two dimensions stay below 6 (Community, Measurement). Both lie outside what this change touches and are named here instead of being scored up.

#### DX implementation checklist

```
DX IMPLEMENTATION CHECKLIST
============================
[x] Time to hello world < 5 min for a repeat start (estimate; first run not measured)
[ ] Installation is one command (three: install, db start, dev; unchanged)
[x] First run produces meaningful output (first board after sign-in; unchanged)
[x] Magical moment delivered via runbook + manifest diff
[x] Every error message has: problem + cause + fix (no docs link: the HINT names the commands)
[x] API naming is guessable without docs (single-column, SELECTABLE_PRESET_IDS, createBoard returns its columns)
[x] Every parameter has a sensible default (createBoard keeps web-app)
[x] Docs have copy-paste examples that actually work (README order, runbook)
[x] Examples show real use cases (the commands of this rollout)
[x] Upgrade path documented (runbook, three failure cases)
[-] Breaking changes have deprecation warnings + codemods (not applicable: one deploy, 2 users)
[x] TypeScript types included (types.ts carries the new function)
[x] Works in CI/CD without special configuration (same workflow; CLI version checked)
[-] Free tier, search in docs, community channel: not applicable to this change
[x] Changelog exists and is maintained (entry through /ship)
```

#### NOT in scope (DX)

- A measured fresh-clone setup time (T11).
- A reverse migration (decision 61).
- Pinning the workflow's CLI version for good (decision 58).
- `CONTRIBUTING.md`, issue templates, setup telemetry: not touched by this change.
- Moving the first-board creation at sign-in to the new function.

#### What already exists (DX)

- `20260212000000_transaction_rpc_functions.sql`: the style for a multi-step write in one function (`SECURITY INVOKER`, fixed `search_path`).
- `20261011011500_repocard_unique_repo_per_user.sql`: a guard whose message says what happened, that nothing changed and what to do; the new guard follows it and adds `DETAIL` and `HINT`.
- `ActionUserError` and the guards in `auth-guard.ts`: the way to return a message meant for the user.
- `pnpm db:start`, `db:stop`, `db:reset`: the wrappers the docs should name.
- `workflow_dispatch` on the production workflow: the re-run after a refusal.
- `docs/plans/`: where earlier plans are committed; the runbook goes next to this plan.

None proposed. Every accepted item is done in this PR; the declined ones are in "NOT in scope" with their reason.

The `NOTICE` reports the same numbers. Counts are the summary; that nothing was lost is shown row by row. Before the migration a manifest is written from the loaded copy: for every entry its id, owner, repository and timestamps; for every `projectinfo` row on an entry its id, the entry's id, `updated_at` and an `md5` over its content columns; for every card its id, board, column, `order` and `updated_at`. After the migration the same columns are read back by id and the two files are compared with `diff`: every entry is a card with the same id, owner, repository and timestamps; every `projectinfo` row is on the card with the entry's id, with the same hash and `updated_at`; every card that existed before is unchanged; the boards that hold the migrated ids are as many as the users who had entries. The destination boards are found through the card ids, not through the board name.\n9. Update every import.\n- `board-data.ts`: delete `getUserMaintenanceRepoIdentifiers` and `maintenanceRepoIdentifiers`; `board/[id]/page.tsx` awaits the board bundle only.\n- `repo-card-duplicates.ts`: placements are board placements only. `RepoPlacements` becomes `BoardPlacement[]` (a wrapper object with one key says nothing); `SkippedReason` loses `'maintenance'`; the placement lookup is one query.\n- `account/page.tsx`: drop the maintenance count query.\n- `board.ts`, `createBoard`: the board and its columns are created in one transaction and returned together. Today the board is inserted first and the columns in a second request; when the second fails, the action reports a failure and leaves a board without columns, and a retry makes a second board. New function `create_board_with_status_lists(p_name text, p_columns jsonb)` in the style of `20260212000000_transaction_rpc_functions.sql` (`SECURITY INVOKER`, fixed `search_path`, owner from `auth.uid()`, position after the user's last board, the grants the other functions have): it inserts the board and one column per element and returns the board with its columns. `createBoard` returns `{ id, name, statusLists }` (id, name and color of each column, the shape the move dialog lists); `/boards/new` keeps reading `id`. A database failure is reported to Sentry and returned as \"The board was not created. Try again.\" (an `ActionUserError`); both callers showed \"An unexpected error occurred\" before. The first-board creation at sign-in keeps its own path.\n- `board-presets.ts`: new preset `single-column` with one column \"Repositories\", the name the migrated board uses. `SELECTABLE_PRESET_IDS` holds the six presets `/boards/new` lists; `PRESET_IDS` is those plus `single-column` and stays the list `presetIdSchema` accepts. `PresetSelector` renders `SELECTABLE_PRESET_IDS`. The docstrings of `createBoard`, `BOARD_PRESETS` and the selector say which list is which.\n\n"},{"oldText":"### 3. Client\n\nRemoval:\n\n- Move the `BoardOption` type from `RestoreToBoardDialog.tsx` into `MoveToAnotherBoardDialog.tsx`, its remaining user. Then delete `src/app/maintenance/**`, `RestoreToBoardDialog` (+ stories), `DeleteMaintenanceDialog`, `ROUTES.MAINTENANCE`.\n- `OverflowMenu`: remove \"Move to Maintenance\", `context`, `onRestoreToBoard`, `onDelete`, the destructive maintenance item and the generic `TId`; ids are `RepoCardId`.\n- Remove the `onMoveToMaintenance`/`onMaintenance` prop chain and `handleMoveToMaintenance`. `useOptimisticCardAction` keeps its remaining caller, Remove from Board; its docstring says so. This default stands unless the engineering review records a different decision.\n- `brands.ts`: delete `MaintenanceId`, `toMaintenanceId`, `CardIdentifier`. `NoteModal`, `useNoteModalDraft` and `draftSlice` use `RepoCardId` instead of a generic id.\n- `HeldRepositoryList`: a held repository is always on another board; the `location` union collapses to the board fields.\n- `AddRepositoryCombobox`: remove `maintenanceRepoIdentifiers` and the page-load fallback set. When placements are unknown, only repositories on the current board are hidden (the server still refuses duplicates by the unique index, with the message that names the holding board; that copy is unchanged).\n- Command Palette: remove \"Go to Maintenance\" and the `G M - Go to Maintenance` part of the shortcuts hint.\n- `/maintenance` returns the app's 404 page. No redirect: nothing links to it and the product has 2 users.\n\nSidebar:\n\n- Remove the \"Maintenance Mode\" item and one of the two dividers that framed it. Order after the change, expanded and collapsed: All Boards, Favorites, one divider, Settings, Shortcuts, theme. No link to the migrated board takes the item's place; it is an ordinary board.\n\nMove to Another Board (`MoveToAnotherBoardDialog`), now the only way to set a repository aside with its notes:\n\n| State | What the user sees |\n|---|---|\n| Loading boards | unchanged |\n| Loading fails | the error text plus a \"Try again\" button that loads again; never the \"no other board\" state |\n| No other board | \"You have no other board yet. Create one to move this repository to.\" A labeled field \"Board name\" and a \"Create board\" button. Creating makes a board with one column \"Repositories\" (a `blank` preset in `board-presets.ts` that `createBoard` accepts and the six-card selector on `/boards/new` does not list), loads the boards again, and selects the new board and its column. The user then presses Move. A failed creation shows its error under the field (`role=\"alert\"`) and keeps the typed name |\n| Destination has no column | the existing message plus a link \"Open <board>\" to add one there |\n| Move fails | unchanged: inline alert, selections kept |\n| Move succeeds | toast \"Moved to <board>\" with an \"Open board\" action that goes to that board |\n\n- Focus: after a board is created, focus goes to the Move button. The field has a visible label, the button reads \"Creating…\" and is disabled while the request runs, Enter in the field submits.\n- The dialog keeps preselecting the first board when there are several.\n\nRemove from Board (confirmation in `OverflowMenu`):\n\n- The text that says the note, links and comment are deleted gains one sentence: \"To keep them, use Move to Another Board instead.\" Separator and destructive styling stay.\n\n`/account`:\n\n- Two tiles in this order: Boards, Repository Cards. `grid-cols-2` at every width (the three tiles were `grid-cols-3` at every width). `account/loading.tsx` shows two skeleton tiles in the same grid, so the page does not jump from three to two.\n\nLanding `FeaturesSection`:\n\n- Fourth card: icon `ArrowRightLeft`, title \"Set Work Aside\", text \"Move finished or paused repositories to a board of their own. Notes, links and comments stay with each card, so nothing is lost when you bring it back.\" The card keeps the promise the old one made and says how it works now. Four cards stay: the grid is two columns wide from `md` up.\n- The \"Project Info\" card stops promising \"secure credentials\"; that feature was removed in `20251231000000_remove_credential_auditlog.sql`. New text: \"Add notes and important links to each repository. Keep all project context in one place.\"\n- The four cards lose `cursor-pointer`: they are not links.\n\n","newText":"### 3. A failed creation leaves no board behind (section 2) and shows the action's message under the field (`role=\"alert\"`): the validation text for a name that is empty or too long, otherwise \"The board was not created. Try again.\" The typed name is kept |\n| Other boards exist, the user wants a new one | a \"New board…\" button under the board field swaps the two selects for the same create form, with \"Choose an existing board\" to go back. After a board is created the selects return with the new board and its column selected |\n| Destination has no column | the existing message plus a link \"Open <board>\" to add one there |\n| Move fails | unchanged: inline alert, selections kept |\n| Move succeeds | toast \"Moved to <board>\" with an \"Open board\" action that goes to that board |\n\n- Focus: after a board is created, focus goes to the Move button. The field has a visible label, the button reads \"Creating…\" and is disabled while the request runs, Enter in the field submits. \"New board…\" moves focus to the name field; \"Choose an existing board\" moves it back to the board select. Four cards stay: the grid is two columns wide from `md` up.\n- The \"Project Info\" card stops promising \"secure credentials\"; that feature was removed in `20251231000000_remove_credential_auditlog.sql`. Tests and fixtures\n\n- Delete the 4 `maintenance-*.spec.ts` files and `MaintenanceClient.test.tsx`.\n- Remove maintenance cases from the other e2e specs and unit tests; keep every case that tests board behavior. `one-repo-across-boards.spec.ts` and the placement unit tests keep the cross-board cases. A case that used a Maintenance entry only as \"some repository held elsewhere\" uses a card on another board instead.\n- `command-palette.spec.ts` uses \"Go to Maintenance\" as the unrelated command that a search for \"boards\" must filter out. It switches to \"Go to Settings\", so the assertion can still fail.\n- `ssr-hydration.spec.ts` lists the landing feature headings; its 'Maintenance Mode' entry becomes the new card's title, so the new card is asserted. `server-rendered-client-components.test.ts` drops its `MaintenanceClient` entry.\n- `seed.sql`: drop maintenance rows, their `projectinfo`, the `maintenance_id` column from inserts and the header lines that list them. `db-query.ts`: drop maintenance helpers. `mocks/handlers`: drop maintenance handlers and data. Stories: drop maintenance variants.\n- New tests, each for behavior that exists after the change. No test asserts that a removed thing is absent: nothing could bring it back by accident, and such a test would itself contain the word the grep forbids.\n  - Unit, move dialog: with no other board the create form is shown, a created board becomes the selected destination and Move is enabled; a failed load shows \"Try again\" and loads again; a destination without a column links to that board; a successful move shows the toast with its \"Open board\" action.\n  - Unit: `createBoard` with the `blank` preset creates one column named \"Repositories\"; the preset selector still lists six presets; the Remove from Board confirmation names Move to Another Board; `/account` shows the Boards and Repository Cards tiles and its loading state shows two; the placement split and the 23505 mapping with board placements only.\n  - E2E: a card moved to another board and back keeps its note (the park and bring back path); the picker lists a repository of another board under \"Already placed elsewhere\".\n  - Migration: no committed test. The rehearsal in section 1 is the check; its counts are recorded in the PR.\n\n","newText":"### 4. Tests and fixtures\n\n- Delete the 4 `maintenance-*.spec.ts` files and `MaintenanceClient.test.tsx`.\n- Remove maintenance cases from the other e2e specs and unit tests; keep every case that tests board behavior. `one-repo-across-boards.spec.ts` and the placement unit tests keep the cross-board cases. A case that used a Maintenance entry only as \"some repository held elsewhere\" uses a card on another board instead.\n- `command-palette.spec.ts` uses \"Go to Maintenance\" as the unrelated command that a search for \"boards\" must filter out. It switches to \"Go to Settings\", so the assertion can still fail.\n- `ssr-hydration.spec.ts` lists the landing feature headings; its 'Maintenance Mode' entry becomes the new card's title, so the new card is asserted. `server-rendered-client-components.test.ts` drops its `MaintenanceClient` entry.\n- `seed.sql`: drop maintenance rows, their `projectinfo`, the `maintenance_id` column from inserts and the header lines that list them. `db-query.ts`: drop maintenance helpers. `mocks/handlers`: drop maintenance handlers and data. Stories: drop maintenance variants.\n- New tests, each for behavior that exists after the change. No test asserts that a removed thing is absent: nothing could bring it back by accident, and such a test would itself contain the word the grep forbids.\n  - Unit, move dialog: with no other board the create form is shown, a created board becomes the selected destination and Move is enabled, and the boards are not loaded a second time; a failed creation shows its message and keeps the typed name; \"New board…\" opens the form when other boards exist and \"Choose an existing board\" closes it; a failed load shows \"Try again\" and loads again; a destination without a column links to that board; a successful move shows the toast with its \"Open board\" action.\n  - Unit: `createBoard` with the `single-column` preset sends one column named \"Repositories\" and returns the created columns; a database failure returns \"The board was not created. Try again.\"; the preset selector still lists six presets; the Remove from Board confirmation names Move to Another Board; `/account` shows the Boards and Repository Cards tiles and its loading state shows two; the placement split and the 23505 mapping with board placements only.\n  - E2E: a card moved to another board and back keeps its note (the park and bring back path); the picker lists a repository of another board under \"Already placed elsewhere\"; a card is moved to a board created inside the dialog, and that board shows its one column \"Repositories\" with the card (this run goes through the real function in the local database, which the unit tests mock).\n  - Migration: no committed test. The rehearsal in section 1 is the check; its counts are recorded in the PR.\n\n"},{"oldText":"### 5. Docs\n\n- `SPEC.md`: remove the Maintenance sections, routes, data model and wireframes; describe archiving as \"a board of your own\".\n- `README.md`, `CLAUDE.md` (schema is 6 tables, route tree, verification rules without Maintenance, the fixture-auth steps that open `/maintenance`, server action list, board data paragraph), `TODOS.md` (delete \"Enforce board vs Maintenance exclusivity in the database\"; reword the entries that mention Maintenance, restore or the restore dialog; add \"Refresh a card's GitHub metadata\": `meta` is a snapshot from the moment a card is added and nothing updates it; add \"Move focus when a card leaves the board\": after Move to Another Board or Remove from Board the focused card is gone and focus falls to the page).\n- Retake `public/screenshots/app-screenshot.png` (1200×670, README) and `public/screenshots/social-preview.png` (1280×640). Both show the owner's \"Laststance\" board in the dark theme with \"Maintenance Mode\" in the sidebar. Same board and theme, taken from the local copy of production running a production build (`pnpm build` and `pnpm start` with the local Supabase URL and anon key, so no dev indicator is in the frame), after the sidebar change; only public repositories in the frame.\n- `CHANGELOG.md` and version through `/ship`.\n\n","newText":"### 5. Docs\n\n- `SPEC.md`: remove the Maintenance sections, routes, data model and wireframes; describe archiving as \"a board of your own\".\n- `README.md`, `CLAUDE.md` (schema is 6 tables, route tree, verification rules without Maintenance, the fixture-auth steps that open `/maintenance`, server action list, board data paragraph), `TODOS.md` (delete \"Enforce board vs Maintenance exclusivity in the database\"; reword the entries that mention Maintenance, restore or the restore dialog; add \"Refresh a card's GitHub metadata\": `meta` is a snapshot from the moment a card is added and nothing updates it; add \"Move focus when a card leaves the board\": after Move to Another Board or Remove from Board the focused card is gone and focus falls to the page).\n- Retake `public/screenshots/app-screenshot.png` (1200×670, README) and `public/screenshots/social-preview.png` (1280×640). Both show the owner's \"Laststance\" board in the dark theme with \"Maintenance Mode\" in the sidebar. Same board and theme, taken from the local copy of production running a production build (`pnpm build` and `pnpm start` with the local Supabase URL and anon key, so no dev indicator is in the frame), with the viewport equal to the image size at device scale factor 1, after the sidebar change; only public repositories in the frame.\n- `CLAUDE.md`, \"Production Migration Procedure\": step 3 tells the reader to link a development project, `jqtxjzdxczqwsrvevmyk`, that no longer exists (`supabase projects list` shows production only, 2026-10-12). It becomes \"test locally with `pnpm db:reset`\". Below it a short list \"Migrations that move or delete data\" is added, in general words: count what will be touched; refuse to run when a row cannot be carried over; one transaction with an early return for a second run; dump the data first; compare production's schema with the repository's; rehearse on a copy; compare a manifest before and after. `SPEC.md` names the dead project in three places (environment table, callback table, procedure); they are corrected the same way. The restart command under \"Local Supabase + GitHub OAuth Setup\" and the line about local development use `pnpm db:stop` and `pnpm db:start`, as the rule above them demands.\n- `README.md`, \"Getting Started\", becomes one ordered path: the pnpm version of `packageManager` (11.1.2; the README says 10.26.2), the GitHub OAuth step (`supabase/.env`) before `pnpm db:start`, which reads that file, and the restart through the scripts. `.env.local.example` starts with `pnpm db:start` instead of bare `supabase start`.\n- `TODOS.md`: the existing entry for repository search across boards gains its new reason: the installed PWA has no find-in-page.\n- `CHANGELOG.md` and version through `/ship`. The entry says what happened to saved repositories: a board named after the old feature, one card per entry, notes kept. History lives there and in the migration file; the four documents the grep covers describe the product as it is.\n\n"},{"oldText":"### 6. Rollout\n\n1. `pnpm test`, `pnpm lint`, `pnpm build`, `pnpm typecheck`, `pnpm e2e:parallel` (the E2E run resets the local database, so it comes first).\n2. Rehearse the migration (section 1, scenarios A and B).\n3. Local check as `ryota-murakami` on `http://localhost:3008`, on the database of scenario B. Sign in first (local GoTrue gives the user a new id), then re-point the copied rows from the stub owner id to the local id, in this order: `board.user_id`, then `repocard.user_id` (its trigger copies the owner from the board and fires only when the card row itself is updated), then `user_settings` and `user_link_presets` (delete the local user's own row first where the key would collide). Before the re-pointing, while the local user still has only its first board: add a repository, open its menu, Move to Another Board, create the destination board in the dialog and finish the move. Then, on the copied data, check: the migrated `Maintenance` board and its notes, Move to Another Board in both directions, Remove from Board and its confirmation text, the picker's \"Already placed elsewhere\" for a repository on the `Maintenance` board, repositories added from the picker to a new board (two columns, one private or organization repository), sidebar (expanded and collapsed), palette, `/account` (also at 320 px wide), the landing page, `/maintenance`. Once with the keyboard only: card menu, move dialog, pick a board, Move. Once at a phone-sized viewport: scroll the `Maintenance` column to a card near its end, open its note, move the card to a working board and back. Then the two screenshots of section 5; before they are committed, each image is looked at once more for a private repository name.\n4. `/review`, `/design-review`, `/ship` (draft PR first; CI runs on PRs only). One PR, three commits that each pass `pnpm typecheck`: (a) server and client removal with their tests, the move dialog link and the landing card; (b) the migration, `types.ts`, the seed and the fixtures; (c) docs and screenshots. The migration is reviewed as its own commit.\n5. Right before the merge: run the audit query on production again and note the `entries` count. Expected `entries_also_on_a_board` and `case_variant_groups`: 0. Nothing writes to Maintenance between this step and the migration run: the owner is the only user with entries and is away, and no check uses Move to Maintenance or Restore on production. Take a data-only dump of production's `public` schema to a local file outside the repository. Confirm that the owner has at least two repositories on no board (the scratch boards of step 9 need them). No check on production or on a preview deployment adds a repository that is in Maintenance to a board before the merge (the new picker does not know about Maintenance, and previews use the production database). If the audit shows an overlap anyway, the migration would refuse to run; remove the board card the check added, then merge.\n6. Merge. The merge runs `supabase db push` on production at once and Vercel deploys.\n7. Wait for the `Supabase Production Migration` run of the merge commit to finish green and read the migration's `NOTICE` in its log. Then run the verification query on production: table and column gone, one board named `Maintenance`, its card count equal to \"entries moved\" in the `NOTICE` and to the count noted in step 5, the `projectinfo` rows on those cards, 2 policies, trigger `O`.\n8. Wait for the Vercel production deployment of the merge commit.\n9. Production check as `ryota-murakami`: board \"Maintenance\" shows its cards, a known repository is found on it with the browser's find-in-page and its note opens, sidebar/palette/account/landing changed, `/maintenance` is 404, the picker lists a repository of the Maintenance board under \"Already placed elsewhere\". On two scratch boards: add repositories, Move to Another Board between them, Remove from Board; delete both boards afterwards. The scratch boards need repositories that are on no board; after the migration every former Maintenance repository is placed. If the picker offers none, the add, move and remove flows are reported as not verified on production, with that reason.\n10. Fill `meta` of the migrated cards. Maintenance never stored it, so those cards show a bare title next to cards that carry a description, stars and language. For each migrated card read the repository with `gh api` as `ryota-murakami` and collect the fields `addRepositoriesToBoard` stores. Write them in one `DO` block through `supabase db query --linked`: the values travel as one JSON document in a dollar-quoted literal (no quote escaping), and `update_repocard_updated_at` is disabled before the UPDATE and enabled after it, so the cards keep their `updated_at`. Tried on the local copy first. By hand, nothing committed. A repository GitHub no longer returns keeps its empty `meta`. Check afterwards: the number of cards on the board with a non-empty `meta` equals the number of repositories GitHub returned, the trigger is enabled, and the board in the browser shows descriptions.\n\nWhen the migration run fails on `lock_timeout`: run it again with `gh workflow run supabase-production.yml` (the workflow has `workflow_dispatch`). When it fails for another reason: the database is unchanged (one transaction) and the failed migration is not recorded, so the fix is an edit of the same migration file in a new commit on `main`, which triggers the run again. Meanwhile the new build runs on the old schema and works; the Maintenance entries are intact and not reachable from the UI until the migration succeeds.\n\nWhen the migration succeeds and the result is wrong: rebuild the affected cards by hand from the rows in the dump of step 5 (its `maintenance` inserts target a table that no longer exists, so the file is a source of values, not something to replay). Point-in-time recovery is the last resort; it also rolls back every other write made since.\n\nKnown window: in earlier runs the migration finished about 35 s after the merge (estimate), and the Vercel build takes a few minutes. Until the new build is live, the old code runs on the new schema. The board page keeps working (`getUserMaintenanceRepoIdentifiers` ignores the query error and returns an empty list), and so do notes, links and comments of board cards (the old code reads and writes them by `repo_card_id` only, `shared-project-info.ts:236` and `:299`). The old Maintenance page shows an empty list, Move to Maintenance fails with an error toast, and the picker's placement lookup shows its error state. A tab opened before the deploy keeps the old client; its server actions belong to the old build, and a reload brings the new one. Accepted (2 users, night rollout); the alternative is two PRs.\n\n","newText":"### 6. Rollout\n\nThe runbook is `docs/plans/2026-10-12-issue-213-rollout-runbook.md`, committed with the PR. It holds, as blocks that can be pasted, every command and SQL statement of the rehearsal (section 1), of the local re-pointing (step 3) and of steps 5, 7 and 10, plus the query that lists guard conflicts. Each block names its input, the expected result and when to stop. It is a document: nothing in it runs in CI or in a test, and no dump, manifest or repository list is committed. The PR body links to it and records the results of this rollout.\n\n1. `pnpm test`, `pnpm lint`, `pnpm build`, `pnpm typecheck`, `pnpm e2e:parallel` (the E2E run resets the local database, so it comes first).\n2. Rehearse the migration (section 1, scenarios A and B).\n3. Local check as `ryota-murakami` on `http://localhost:3008`, on the database of scenario B. Sign in first (local GoTrue gives the user a new id), then re-point the copied rows from the stub owner id to the local id, in this order: `board.user_id`, then `repocard.user_id` (its trigger copies the owner from the board and fires only when the card row itself is updated), then `user_settings` and `user_link_presets` (delete the local user's own row first where the key would collide). Before the re-pointing, while the local user still has only its first board: add a repository, open its menu, Move to Another Board, create the destination board in the dialog and finish the move. Then, on the copied data, check: the migrated `Maintenance` board and its notes, Move to Another Board in both directions, Remove from Board and its confirmation text, the picker's \"Already placed elsewhere\" for a repository on the `Maintenance` board, repositories added from the picker to a new board (two columns, one private or organization repository), sidebar (expanded and collapsed), palette, `/account` (also at 320 px wide), the landing page, `/maintenance`. Once with the keyboard only: card menu, move dialog, pick a board, Move. Once at a phone-sized viewport: scroll the `Maintenance` column to a card near its end, open its note, move the card to a working board and back. Then the two screenshots of section 5; before they are committed, each image is looked at once more for a private repository name.\n4. `/review`, `/design-review`, `/ship` (draft PR first; CI runs on PRs only). One PR. Its commits each pass `pnpm typecheck` and keep removal and new behavior apart: (a) removal of the feature from client and server with its tests, stories and fixtures, the move of `shared-project-info.ts` recorded as a rename; (b) the removal migration with `types.ts` and the seed; (c) board creation in one transaction (function migration, `createBoard`, the `single-column` preset) and the move dialog states with their tests; (d) sidebar divider, `/account` and landing; (e) docs, runbook and screenshots. The exact cut is made while implementing. Two rules hold: a commit that deletes does not also add behavior, and a migration shares its commit with nothing but its types and seed.\n5. Right before the merge: run the audit query on production again and note the `entries` count. Expected `entries_also_on_a_board` and `case_variant_groups`: 0. Nothing writes to Maintenance between this step and the migration run: the owner is the only user with entries and is away, and no check uses Move to Maintenance or Restore on production. Take a data-only dump of production's `public` schema to a local file outside the repository, and write the manifest of section 1, step 8 from production as JSON next to it (it is also the readable copy of what the table held). The directory is made with `install -d -m 700` and the files are written under `umask 077`, so they are at no moment readable by another account. Confirm that the owner has at least two repositories on no board (the scratch boards of step 9 need them). No check on production or on a preview deployment adds a repository that is in Maintenance to a board before the merge (the new picker does not know about Maintenance, and previews use the production database). If the audit shows an overlap anyway, the migration would refuse to run; remove the board card the check added, then merge.\n6. Merge. The merge runs `supabase db push` on production at once and Vercel deploys.\n7. Wait for the `Supabase Production Migration` run of the merge commit to finish green and read the migration's `NOTICE` in its log. Then run the verification query on production: table and column gone, one board named `Maintenance`, its card count equal to \"entries moved\" in the `NOTICE` and to the count noted in step 5, the `projectinfo` rows on those cards, 2 policies, trigger `O`. Then read the manifest columns back by id and compare them with the manifest of step 5: the diff is empty.\n8. Wait for the Vercel production deployment of the merge commit.\n9. Production check as `ryota-murakami`: board \"Maintenance\" shows its cards, a known repository is found on it with the browser's find-in-page and its note opens, sidebar/palette/account/landing changed, `/maintenance` is 404, the picker lists a repository of the Maintenance board under \"Already placed elsewhere\". On two scratch boards: add repositories, Move to Another Board between them, Remove from Board; delete both boards afterwards. The scratch boards need repositories that are on no board; after the migration every former Maintenance repository is placed. If the picker offers none, the add, move and remove flows are reported as not verified on production, with that reason.\n10. Fill `meta` of the migrated cards. Maintenance never stored it, so those cards show a bare title next to cards that carry a description, stars and language. For each migrated card read the repository with `gh api` as `ryota-murakami` and collect the fields `addRepositoriesToBoard` stores. Write them in one `DO` block through `supabase db query --linked`: the values travel as one JSON document in a dollar-quoted literal (no quote escaping), and `update_repocard_updated_at` is disabled before the UPDATE and enabled after it, so the cards keep their `updated_at`. Tried on the local copy first. By hand: the commands are in the runbook, the values are not committed. The step starts by confirming that `gh` acts as `ryota-murakami`. A repository GitHub no longer returns keeps its empty `meta`. Check afterwards: the number of cards on the board with a non-empty `meta` equals the number of repositories GitHub returned, the trigger is enabled, and the board in the browser shows descriptions.\n\nWhen the migration run fails, the log says which of three cases it is. On `lock_timeout`: run it again with `gh workflow run supabase-production.yml` (the workflow has `workflow_dispatch`). On a refusal (the message starts with `remove_maintenance_mode:`): the migration is right and the data or the schema is not what it expects; list the conflicts with the runbook query, resolve them as the `HINT` says and run the workflow again, without a commit. On any other error the migration has a defect: the database is unchanged (one transaction) and the failed migration is not recorded, so the fix is an edit of the same migration file in a new commit on `main`, which triggers the run again. Meanwhile the new build runs on the old schema and works, board creation included (its function is a migration of its own that ran first); the Maintenance entries are intact and not reachable from the UI until the migration succeeds. Only when the function migration itself fails does the new build answer board creation with its error message until the fix is pushed.\n\nWhen the migration succeeds and the result is wrong: rebuild the affected cards by hand from the rows in the dump of step 5 (its `maintenance` inserts target a table that no longer exists, so the file is a source of values, not something to replay). The manifest comparison of step 7 names the rows that are missing or different. Point-in-time recovery is the last resort; it also rolls back every other write made since.\n\nKnown window: in earlier runs the migration finished about 35 s after the merge (estimate), and the Vercel build takes a few minutes. Until the new build is live, the old code runs on the new schema. The board page keeps working (`getUserMaintenanceRepoIdentifiers` ignores the query error and returns an empty list), and so do notes, links and comments of board cards (the old code reads and writes them by `repo_card_id` only, `shared-project-info.ts:236` and `:299`). The old Maintenance page shows an empty list, Move to Maintenance fails with an error toast, and the picker's placement lookup shows its error state. A tab opened before the deploy keeps the old client; its server actions belong to the old build, and a reload brings the new one. Accepted (2 users, night rollout); the alternative is two PRs.\n\n"},{"oldText":"## Out of scope\n\n- A board preset or a special board type for archiving. <!-- autoplan-accepted:dx -->

- Board creation is one transaction: `createBoard` creates the board and its columns through one database function and returns the columns, and a failed creation leaves no board. Unit tests cover the returned columns and the failure message; one E2E test creates a board from the move dialog against the local database.
- The preset the move dialog uses is named `single-column` (the design requirement above calls it `blank`). `SELECTABLE_PRESET_IDS` is what `/boards/new` renders; `PRESET_IDS` is what `createBoard` accepts.
- The move dialog does not load the boards again after it created one, and it offers "New board…" also when other boards exist.
- The removal migration refuses with a labeled message, `DETAIL` and `HINT` that carry counts only. It refuses as well when `projectinfo` has any policy set other than the two known names, and it drops those two by name.
- Before the rehearsal, production's `public` schema is compared with the repository's at `20261011030000`. The objects the migration touches show no difference, or the difference is resolved and recorded in the PR body.
- That nothing was lost is shown by a manifest compared before and after (ids, owners, repositories, timestamps, content hashes), in the rehearsal and on production. The comparison finds the destination boards through card ids.
- `docs/plans/2026-10-12-issue-213-rollout-runbook.md` is committed. It holds every command and statement of the rehearsal, the local re-pointing, the checks before the merge, the verification, the conflict listing and the metadata fill, each with its expected result. No dump, manifest or repository list is committed.
- The rehearsal runs on the Supabase CLI version the production workflow installs; both versions are in the PR body.
- `CLAUDE.md` and `SPEC.md` no longer point at the deleted development project. `README.md` gives one ordered setup path with the pinned pnpm version and the script commands.
- Commits keep removal and new behavior apart, and each passes `pnpm typecheck`.

What existing code already solves each part:

| List destinations                               | `getUserBoardsWithStatusLists` (`repo-cards.ts`)                                                                | reused; a created board is appended on the client |

Minimum change: the issue asks for the removal; the board creation function and the dialog states come from the CEO, design and DX phases, where both voices asked for them. Scope is not reduced (override rule, P2).

Complexity check: the change touches far more than 8 files and adds one database function, one hook and one component. The gate would stop here in an interactive run. It is passed on the owner's direction of 2026-10-12 (one PR, scope is not a constraint), and the removal itself is deletion: the diff adds less than it takes away.

Search check. Aside is not installed and no web search ran for this phase; the patterns are built-ins of tools already in the repository. [Layer 1] `jsonb_array_elements … WITH ORDINALITY` gives the array position inside one `INSERT … SELECT`. [Layer 1] Sonner's `action` option renders the "Open board" button of the toast. [Layer 1] The dialog's `onOpenChange` already decides whether a close request is honored, so "cannot be closed while pending" needs no new primitive. [Layer 1] `RAISE EXCEPTION … USING DETAIL = …, HINT = …` is standard PL/pgSQL; what the Supabase CLI prints of it is checked in the rehearsal.

Nothing in `TODOS.md` blocks this plan. The entry about writes that are not counted yet (column create, rename and delete, adding repositories, comment edits) is untouched; Move to Another Board is counted by this plan and does not join that list.

Completeness check: every gap found below is closed in the plan, none is deferred. Distribution check: no new artifact is built or published.

#### Step 0.5: dual voices

CODEX SAYS (eng, architecture challenge). Completed in 298 s, 11 findings (2 High, 8 Medium, 1 Low), verdict "revise before merge":

1. High. The function's contract does not say how columns are positioned. `createStatusListsFromPreset` writes `order`, `grid_row` and `grid_col`; `KanbanBoard` places columns by the grid fields; a function that inserts name and color only stacks every column in one cell, and the planned single-column tests pass anyway. No spec submits `/boards/new`.
2. High. Move to Another Board is not a counted write, and a late answer can remove the card from the destination board's client state: start a slow move, dismiss the dialog, open the destination, then the answer arrives and `removeRepoCard` runs on the board now in the store.
3. Medium. While the create form is shown, the dialog still derives a destination (the first board), so Move stays enabled for a board the user no longer sees.
4. Medium. The function is a callable API: its checks, its return shape and its `types.ts` entry are undefined, and a mocked `.rpc()` error proves the message, not the rollback.
5. Medium. The migration's lock order is the reverse of `restore_to_board`; a running call can deadlock with it (`40P01`), and the plan would call that a defect of the migration.
6. Medium. A card reuses its entry's id, but nothing checks that no card already has that id.
7. Medium. The local re-pointing can fail after the boards moved: the repository added before it may also be on a copied board.
8. Medium. "The new build runs on the old schema and works" is too strong: there are three schema states, and on the middle one the new picker lets a Maintenance repository onto a board. Also the old build cannot add repositories on the new schema at all.
9. Medium. Comparing versions on the day does not make production run the rehearsed CLI; pin it for the rollout and its retries.
10. Medium. The metadata fill is verified by a count, which passes with swapped values or wrong keys; failed requests to GitHub are not told apart from repositories that are gone.
11. Low. `account-profile.spec.ts:53` expects 3 tiles.

It found the removal sequence itself coherent: board and column before cards, both foreign keys of `projectinfo` in one statement, the trigger toggle, and the drop order of foreign key, policies and table.

CLAUDE SUBAGENT (eng, independent review). Completed in 407 s, 15 findings (3 Medium, 12 Low), no blocker; it calls the data-safety design sound:

- M1 the workflow installs the CLI as `latest`: pin it and leave it pinned. M2 the commit rules contradict each other (the function migration shares a commit with code), and `types.ts` needs the new function's signature. M3 the manifest diff reports a user's write between manifest and migration as a difference.
- L1 Move to Another Board is not a counted write. L2 the function needs checks and `REVOKE` from `PUBLIC` and `anon`. L3 `createBoard` inserts the untrimmed name. L4 the dialog is heading for a dozen `useState` calls: extract a hook and a form component. L5 "the same two clicks" is wrong; remember the last destination. L6 scope the metadata fill by id and assert its row count. L7 the first board at sign-in keeps the two-request write; moving it is a few lines. L8 a preview cannot create boards before the merge. L9 the `NOTICE` in the job log is untested. L10 the dumps need a deletion date. L11 wording. L12 one long column is fine at 23 cards.
- It confirmed against code: the two policies, the column constraints, that only the two functions reference the table, the precedent migration, that `database.types.ts` has one importer, the old build's project info path, and the isolation of E2E shards. It answered the open question of the DX phase: `useOptimisticCardAction` stays as it is.
